1	# Build New Preview Workflow
2	
3	**Goal:** Turn user intent into a hardened, reviewable artifact.
4	
5	**CRITICAL:** If a step directs you to another snapshot file, read it fully and follow it. No exceptions.
6	
7	Subagents, when the capability is available, are an important part of this workflow. Use them as directed by the workflow steps.
8	If you need an explicit user instruction to run them, ask once now for the whole workflow run.
9	
10	## READY FOR DEVELOPMENT STANDARD
11	
12	A specification is "Ready for Development" when:
13	
14	- **Actionable**: Every task has a file path and specific action.
15	- **Logical**: Tasks ordered by dependency.
16	- **Testable**: All ACs use Given/When/Then.
17	- **Complete**: No placeholders or TBDs.
18	- **Sufficient**: No known requirement, acceptance, dependency, or implementation gaps remain unresolved.
19	- **Coherent**: No unresolved ambiguities or internal contradictions.
20	
21	## SCOPE STANDARD
22	
23	A specification should target a **single user-facing goal** within **900–1600 tokens**:
24	
25	- **Single goal**: One cohesive feature, even if it spans multiple layers/files. Multi-goal means >=2 **top-level independent shippable deliverables** — each could be reviewed, tested, and merged as a separate PR without breaking the others. Never count surface verbs, "and" conjunctions, or noun phrases. Never split cross-layer implementation details inside one user goal.
26	  - Split: "add dark mode toggle AND refactor auth to JWT AND build admin dashboard"
27	  - Don't split: "add validation and display errors" / "support drag-and-drop AND paste AND retry"
28	- **900–1600 tokens**: Optimal range for LLM consumption. Below 900 risks ambiguity; above 1600 risks context-rot in implementation agents.
29	- **Neither limit is a gate.** Both are proposals with user override.
30	
31	## Conventions
32	
33	- Every operational cross-file reference in this workflow is an absolute snapshot path. Open it directly; do not resolve it relative to a skill directory.
34	- `{project-root}`-prefixed paths resolve from the project working directory.
35	- Whenever this workflow captures or records a version-control revision, obtain the full canonical identifier directly from version control and preserve it verbatim.
36	
37	## On Activation
38	
39	### Step 1: Execute Prepend Steps
40	
41	Execute each of these steps in order before proceeding (`_None._` means skip):
42	
43	_None._
44

46	
47	Treat every entry below as foundational context you carry for the rest of the workflow run. Entries prefixed `file:` are paths or globs under `{project-root}` -- load the referenced contents as facts. All other entries are facts verbatim (`_None._` means none):
48	
49	_None._
50	
51	### Step 3: Execute Append Steps
52	
53	Execute each of these steps in order (`_None._` means skip):
54	
55	_None._
56	
57	## WORKFLOW ARCHITECTURE
58	
59	This uses **step-file architecture** for disciplined execution:
60	
61	- **Micro-file Design**: Each step is self-contained and followed exactly
62	- **Just-In-Time Loading**: Only load the current step file
63	- **Sequential Enforcement**: Complete steps in order, no skipping
64	- **State Tracking**: Persist progress via spec frontmatter and in-memory variables
65	- **Append-Only Building**: Build artifacts incrementally
66	
67	### Step Processing Rules
68	
69	1. **READ COMPLETELY**: Read the entire step file before acting
70	2. **FOLLOW SEQUENCE**: Execute sections in order
71	3. **WAIT FOR INPUT**: Halt at checkpoints and wait for human
72	4. **LOAD NEXT**: When directed, read fully and follow the next step file
73	
74	### Critical Rules (NO EXCEPTIONS)
75	
76	- **NEVER** load multiple step files simultaneously
77	- **ALWAYS** read entire step file before execution
78	- **NEVER** skip steps or optimize the sequence
79	- **ALWAYS** follow the exact instructions in the step file
80	- **ALWAYS** halt at checkpoints and wait for human input

<frozen-after-approval reason="human-owned intent — do not modify unless human renegotiates">

## Intent

**Problem:** The `/` route shows only an "All parks" heading. A resident cannot browse parks or open one without a map, and the list is the main way to choose a park (CAP-2).

**Approach:** Replace the list placeholder with an "All parks" heading, a one-line hint, and a `<ul>` of 12 park cards, sorted A–Z by the existing loader. Each card is a single React Router `<Link>` to `/parks/:id`. When the user comes back to `/` from a park, focus returns to that park's card.

## Boundaries & Constraints

**Always:** use the normalized parks from `useParks` as they are (order and `number` come from the loader, never recomputed); never invent data; one Tab stop per card; amber `focus-ring` on every card; touch target ≥ 44px; Tailwind tokens from `src/index.css`.

**Never:** details content, the "All parks" back control or focus-to-heading on details (Story 1.3); map or markers (1.4); desktop sidebar/phone toggle layout (1.5); clickable `<div>`s; a nested interactive element inside a card.

## I/O & Edge-Case Matrix

| Scenario | Input / State | Expected Output / Behavior | Error Handling |
|----------|--------------|---------------------------|----------------|
| Happy path | 12 sample parks | 12 `<li>` cards in loader order, badges 1–12 | N/A |
| Missing address | Highland Dog Park `address: null` | "Address not listed" in `text-muted` | N/A |
| > 3 amenities | Prospect Park (7) | first 3 tags + "+4 more" | N/A |
| ≤ 3 amenities | East Ridge (3) | 3 tags, no "+N more" | N/A |
| No amenities | `amenities: []` | no tag row | N/A |
| Amenity label | `dog-run`, `wifi` | "Dog run", "Wifi" | unknown tokens humanized, never dropped |
| Return from details | `/parks/highland-dog-park` → Back | Highland card has focus | N/A |
| First visit | open `/` directly | no card is auto-focused | N/A |

</frozen-after-approval>

## Code Map

- `src/App.jsx` -- `ParkListPlaceholder` (renders only the h1) is what this story replaces; `ParksPanel` owns the `<Routes>` and receives `parks`; `ParkDetailsPlaceholder` renders on `/parks/:id`. Keep the loading/error states, `NotFound`, `cardClass`, and the landmarks.
- `src/data/parks.js` -- normalized park: `{ id, name, number, address, amenities, ... }`; `amenities` is always an array of strings. Don't change normalization.
- `src/index.css` -- tokens (`tag-bg`, `tag-fg`, `muted`, `forest`, `line`, `surface`) and the `focus-ring` utility.
- `src/App.test.jsx` -- `renderAt(path)` + `okResponse()` + the `TestNav` pattern for history Back; extend it here.
- `design/screens/01-desktop-list-map.png`, `04-phone-list.png` -- card visual: forest round number badge, 17px bold name, muted address, rounded tags. Hint copy: "Choose a park to see hours, amenities and directions."

## Tasks & Acceptance

**Execution:**
- [x] `src/data/format.js` -- `amenityLabel(token)`: replace `-` with spaces, capitalize the first letter, keep the rest; shared with 1.3's details.
- [x] `src/components/ParkList.jsx` -- `ParkList({ parks, focusParkId })`: h1 "All parks", hint `<p>`, `<ul>` of `<li><Link to={`/parks/${id}`}>` cards (badge, name, address or muted fallback, up to 3 tags + "+N more"). On mount, if `focusParkId` matches a card, focus that link.
- [x] `src/App.jsx` -- keep the last-opened park id in App-level state (set from the details route), pass it to `ParkList`, and clear it after the list has used it. Delete `ParkListPlaceholder`.
- [x] `src/data/format.test.js`, `src/App.test.jsx` -- unit-test `amenityLabel`; test every matrix row, Tab reaching each card link, Enter and click navigating to details, and focus returning via history Back.

**Acceptance Criteria:**
- Given the list, when a keyboard user presses Tab, then each card receives focus once, as a link, with the `focus-ring` class.
- Given a focused card, when the user presses Enter, then the URL becomes `/parks/:id` and that park's heading renders.
- Given a card, when the user clicks or taps it, then the app navigates to that park's details.
- Given the list renders, then all 12 parks appear inside a single `<ul>` under the "All parks" h1 and its hint.

**Rejected:**
- Null `name` renders empty card title (BH/EC) — low, unlikely: `name` required by `park-data.md`, all 12 have one; same call as pass-1 BH1/EC2.
- Null/duplicate `id` → duplicate keys, `/parks/null` (EC) — low, unlikely: `id` required, sample ids unique; same as pass-1 BH2/EC1.
- Park id with `/ ? # %` needs `encodeURIComponent` (EC) — low, unlikely: all sample ids are kebab-case slugs.
- Duplicate amenity tokens shown twice, "+N more" inflated (EC) — low, unlikely: no sample park repeats a token; dedupe adds logic.
- Focus restored on any return to `/`, not only Back (BH/EC) — low, unlikely: no in-app link from details to `/` yet; same as pass-1 BH5/EC6.
- `useEffect(..., [])` fails exhaustive-deps; StrictMode double run (BH) — false: no ESLint in project; second run focuses the same card and re-clears null.
- `ParkList` doc comment misleading about order (BH) — false: comment says "loader order with loader numbers", which is exactly what it does.
- No empty state for zero parks (BH) — low, unlikely: same as pass-1 BH7/EC5.
- Long accessible name per card (BH) — rejected by design: Design Notes accept it; changing it edits the spec.
- Unmatched `focusParkId` / focus-visible ring after programmatic focus (BH/EC) — maybe-false, low at most: would need a real-browser check of `:focus-visible` after Back; rejected as low.
- `amenityLabel('')` returns empty pill (BH) — low, unlikely: `stringList` filters blanks before any caller.
- Missing test assertions: badge aria-hidden, invalid id leaves `lastParkId` null, click test not checking list gone (BH) — false/low: removing `aria-hidden` already breaks the `^N\. ` assertion (VG); the other two are negligible.
- Enter/click tests never assert the URL (AA) — false: `ParkDetailsPlaceholder` renders the park h1 only on `/parks/:id`, so the heading proves the route.
- `focus-ring` asserted on one card only (AA) — false: every card comes from the same `ParkCard` className; one card proves all.
- No 13th Tab to rule out an extra stop (AA) — false: card markup has no other focusables (chevron is `aria-hidden` svg).
- Order checked against hard-coded A–Z list (AA) — false: sample JSON is unsorted, so the test observes the loader's sort (VG).
- `onFocusUsed` fires even when no card matched (AA) — false as defect: matches the Design Notes "read once, then clear" intent.
- Commit claims "tests for every list edge case" (EC) — low: the untested edges are the rejected unlikely ones above; no code change.

## Implementation Notes

- `ParkList` also takes `onFocusUsed`, which the parent uses to clear the remembered id after the list consumes it.
- The number badge is `aria-hidden`, with an `sr-only` "N." in its place. Literal spaces sit between the card's text parts, so the accessible name reads "1. Cedar Hill Nature Preserve Cedar Hill Rd Trails …" and not run-together words.
- Decorative chevron per card (`aria-hidden`), from the phone design.
- The details route records the id only for a park that exists.

## Design Notes

Focus return has to work with browser Back, which carries no router state, so the id of the park the user came from lives in App state rather than in `location.state`. The details route records the id on mount. The list reads it once on mount, focuses that card, then clears it, so a later fresh visit to `/` does not steal focus. Story 1.3's "All parks" control will rely on the same mechanism (epic readiness decision).

The card's accessible name is all of its text (number, name, address, tags). That is acceptable for one link per card and matches the design's single-element card.

## Verification

**Commands:**
- `npm test` -- expected: all tests pass.
- `npm run build` -- expected: succeeds with no errors.

**Manual checks (if no CLI):**
- `npm run dev`, Tab through the list: amber ring on each card; Enter opens details; browser Back puts focus back on that card.

# Epic 1 Context: Find a Park

<!-- Compiled from planning artifacts. Edit freely. Regenerate with compile-epic-context if planning docs change. -->

## Goal

Deliver a small, keyless web app (a 2-hour take-home) that lets a resident find a local park in the NYC-area sample set on a map or in a list and open its details, on phone or desktop, by mouse, touch, or keyboard, with missing data handled honestly. The interviewer must be able to run it with one install and one start command and judge it from the README. A modest app that works and is well explained beats an ambitious one with broken core features; stop at 2 hours and document what is unfinished.

## Stories

- Story 1.1: Scaffold app and park data layer
- Story 1.2: Park list with keyboard selection
- Story 1.3: Park details with missing-data handling
- Story 1.4: Map of parks synced with selection
- Story 1.5: Responsive and accessibility polish with design applied
- Story 1.6: Tests and submission README

## Requirements & Constraints

- **Cross-cutting (every story):** no geolocation ever requested; no credentials or API keys; never invent missing data; `parks.sample.json` is never edited (copied into the app byte-for-byte); keyboard focus always visible.
- The list plus details view must be a complete alternative to the map. All 12 parks appear in the list, including ones with missing fields; parks without valid lat/lng get no marker but stay in the list and details.
- Every one of the 12 parks renders; null/missing/empty fields are omitted or labelled, never crash the UI. Failed images degrade without a broken-image icon.
- A data-load failure shows an error message with retry, never a blank screen.
- At 375px and desktop widths, list → details → back works with no horizontal scroll.
- Unredacted AI transcripts (Claude Code, exported by the developer before each fresh story context, and Claude Design) and design files are part of the submission; the README must say where they live.
- Non-goals: backend, accounts, deployment, data editing. Search, filters, current location only if core is done with time to spare.
- Where the design and the spec disagree, the spec wins.

## Technical Decisions

- **Stack:** React + Vite, Tailwind CSS (mobile-first), `react-leaflet` + Leaflet with OpenStreetMap standard tiles (keyless, attribution visible), Vitest + React Testing Library. No component library.
- **Data loading (readiness decision):** the spec says static import, but loading/error/retry states require a mechanism that can actually fail. Use one that can (e.g. `fetch` from `public/` or a dynamic `import()`) and document the choice in the README.
- **Routing (readiness decision):** React Router. `/` is the list, `/parks/:id` is details (supersedes the design's `?park=` option). Unknown id shows "park not found" with a way back. Browser Back must work.
- **Data shape:** array of park objects with `id`, `name` (treat as required); `description`, `location.{lat,lng,address}`, `amenities` (kebab-case tokens), `hours` (free text, shown as written), `images` (URL array), `acreage`, `rating` (0–5) may each be missing, null, or empty. Read nested fields null-safely (`park.location?.address`).
- **Normalization:** one null-safe module applies safe defaults with no fabricated values, sorts parks A–Z and numbers them 1–N; that number is shared by list cards and map markers. Centralize fallback rules in a small helper (e.g. `display(value, fallback)`).
- **Image URL transformation:** pixel art moves from `design/park-images/` to `src/assets/park-images/`. At load time each image URL maps by filename stem to the bundled asset (`.../prospect-1.jpg` → `park-images/prospect-1.png`); unmatched URLs pass through unchanged so `onError` fallback still applies. Do not mutate source data. Document in README.
- **Amenity labels:** replace `-` with a space and capitalize the first letter (`dog-run` → "Dog run"); unknown tokens are humanized, never dropped.
- **Map:** initial view fits bounds of parks with coordinates. Leaflet default marker icons need explicit asset imports under Vite (or use `L.divIcon` with a `<button>` inside). Markers are focusable, named "N. Park name"; the selected marker is amber and larger.
- **Tailwind tokens:** paper `#F6F4EE`, surface `#FFFFFF`, line `#DAD6CB`, ink `#1B2620`, muted `#4F5B54`, forest `#1E5B3F` / dark `#123826`, tag bg `#E9EFE7` / fg `#23472F`, amber `#B45309`, missing bg `#F0EDE5` / line `#C9C4B6`. Font: Public Sans (Google Fonts, 400–800). Focus ring: `focus-visible:outline focus-visible:outline-[3px] focus-visible:outline-amber focus-visible:outline-offset-2`.
- **Tests must cover** every known data gap (see UX section), a failing image, an unmatched image URL, the URL transformation without mutation, and the keyboard list → details → back flow.

## UX & Interaction Patterns

- **Landmarks:** `<header>`, `<main>`, `<aside aria-label="Parks">`, map region labelled "Map of parks". One `<h1>` per view; no clickable `<div>`s; icon-only buttons have `aria-label`s; touch targets ≥ 44×44px; text contrast ≥ 4.5:1.
- **Desktop (≥ 768px):** green header (app name "Find a Park", subtitle, park count); ~400px independently scrolling sidebar; map fills the rest. Selecting a park switches the sidebar to details, starting with an "All parks" back control.
- **Phone (< 768px):** single column with a List / Map toggle of two `<button>`s using `aria-pressed`, List default. Tapping a marker shows a preview card ("Park N of 12", name, address, hours, "View park details", close button "Close park preview"). Details take the full screen with an "All parks" back link.
- **Focus management:** opening details moves focus to the details heading; returning to the list moves focus to the originating card.
- **Park card:** single `<button>`/link per card (one Tab stop) in a `<ul>`; number badge, name (17px bold), address, first three amenities as tags plus "+N more".
- **Details order:** back control, photo area ("Photo 1 of N", alt text naming the park), name as `<h1>` (26–28px, 800), address, "Open in OpenStreetMap" link from lat/lng, `<dl>` fact tiles (Hours, Size in acres, Rating "4.7 / 5"), About, all amenities as checked tags.
- **Missing-data copy:** address → "Address not listed" (muted); description null → "No description has been provided for this park."; rating null → "Not yet rated" (never 0/empty stars); empty or failed images → dashed box "No photos for this park yet"; hours/acreage → "Not listed"; empty amenities → hide section or "No amenities listed"; no lat/lng → no marker, no OSM link. Missing fact tiles use the muted style (missing bg, dashed border, italic), as on the phone screen; the desktop mock's normal-style "Not yet rated" tile is a mistake.
- **Sample gaps:** Old Mill Botanical Garden (null description), Cedar Hill Nature Preserve (empty images, null rating; coordinates look like a placeholder, note in Known issues), East Ridge Trailhead (empty images), Highland Dog Park (missing address).
- **Loading/error states:** "Loading parks…" and "Couldn't load parks. Try again." with a retry button, using the card style.
- Visual reference: `design/screens/` (1280×800 and 390×844). `design/source/*.dc.html` is reference only, not app code.

## Cross-Story Dependencies

- 1.1 provides the data layer, routing, layout shell, tokens, and bundled images that all later stories build on.
- 1.2 needs the 1.3 back control for its focus-return-to-card criterion. **Readiness decision:** in 1.2, verify focus return using browser Back; 1.3 confirms it via the "All parks" control.
- 1.4 relies on the shared A–Z numbering from 1.1 and the selected-park route to highlight markers and open details.
- 1.5 is the full responsive/accessibility pass over 1.2–1.4 (which ship baseline accessibility).
- 1.6 tests cover behaviour from 1.1–1.3; the README records decisions from all stories, including the data-load mechanism and React Router choice.
## Code Map

- Greenfield repo: only `README.md`, `.gitignore` (already ignores `node_modules`, `dist`; `public` is not ignored), `design/`, `requirements/`, and transcripts. Do not touch `design/screens`, `design/source`, the transcripts or `_bmad*`.
- `requirements/parks.sample.json` -- source data; copy it to `public/data/parks.sample.json`.
- `design/park-images/*.png` (12 files) -- `git mv` them to `src/assets/park-images/`; the stems match all 12 sample URLs.
- `design/README.md` -- tokens, font, focus-ring classes, landmark names, loading/error copy.
- Toolchain: Node 25, npm 11. Current releases: vite 8, @vitejs/plugin-react 6, tailwindcss + @tailwindcss/vite 4 (CSS `@theme`, no config file), react 19, react-router 8, vitest 5.

## Tasks & Acceptance

**Execution:**
- [x] `package.json`, `vite.config.js`, `index.html` -- Vite + React + `@tailwindcss/vite`; scripts `dev`, `build`, `test` (`vitest run`); add the Vitest jsdom config and RTL setup; load Public Sans 400–800 from Google Fonts in `index.html`; set the title to "Find a Park".
- [x] `src/index.css` -- `@import "tailwindcss"`; put the `@theme` colour tokens and font from `design/README.md` here and define a `focus-ring` utility with the amber focus-visible outline.
- [x] `public/data/parks.sample.json` -- byte copy of the requirements file.
- [x] `src/assets/park-images/` -- moved pixel art.
- [x] `src/data/parks.js` -- `normalizePark`, `normalizeParks` (sort A–Z, assign `number`), `resolveImageUrl` (stem lookup via `import.meta.glob(..., { eager: true, query: '?url', import: 'default' })`), `loadParks()` (fetch `${import.meta.env.BASE_URL}data/parks.sample.json`, throw on non-OK). Normalized shape: `{ id, name, number, description, address, lat, lng, hasCoords, amenities, hours, images, acreage, rating }`. Use `null` for missing scalars and `[]` for missing arrays.
- [x] `src/data/useParks.js` -- hook returning `{ status: 'loading'|'error'|'ready', parks, retry }`.
- [x] `src/App.jsx`, `src/main.jsx` -- `BrowserRouter`; routes `/` and `/parks/:id`. The shell renders `<header>` (app name, park count once ready), `<main>`, `<aside aria-label="Parks">` (placeholder: the "All parks" heading on `/`; the park name, or "Park not found" with a link back, on `/parks/:id`) and `<section aria-label="Map of parks">` (placeholder). Loading and error states render inside the aside.
- [x] `src/data/parks.test.js`, `src/App.test.jsx` -- unit-test every I/O matrix row, including that the source object is not mutated; test the loading → error → retry → ready flow by mocking `fetch`.

**Acceptance Criteria:**
- Given a fresh clone, when `npm install` and `npm run dev` run, then the app serves locally with no keys.
- Given the app at `/parks/prospect-park`, when the user presses browser Back after navigating from `/`, then `/` renders again.
- Given `public/data/parks.sample.json`, when compared with `requirements/parks.sample.json`, then the bytes are identical.

## Implementation Notes

- vitest 4.1 + jsdom 29 instead of vitest 5 + jsdom 30: the latter declare engines that exclude Node 25 (odd release), so plain `npm install` on Node 25 picks v4/v29 and installs warning-free. Upgrade when on Node 24 LTS or 26.
- Park PNGs are under Vite's 4 KB inline limit, so `?url` yields data URIs in the production build (bundled, no separate files).
- Tests read the fixture with `process.cwd()` because jsdom gives `import.meta.url` an http scheme.
- Normalization also treats empty/whitespace strings as `null` and out-of-range `acreage` (< 0) as `null`; non-object entries are skipped.

## Review Triage Log

Pass 1 (blind-hunter BH, edge-case-hunter EC, verification-gap VG).

| # | Finding | Verdict | Evidence | Route |
|---|---------|---------|----------|-------|
| EC1 | Path matching no route (`/foo`, `/parks/`) renders an empty aside | medium | `<Routes>` in `src/App.jsx` has only `/` and `/parks/:id`; any other path leaves the Parks region blank with no way back | patch |
| EC2/BH8 | Park with `name: null` renders an empty `<h1>` | low | Real in `ParkDetailsPlaceholder`, but `park-data.md` treats `name` as required and all 12 have one; a fallback label would add a branch and risk inventing a value | reject (unlikely) |
| EC3/BH7 | Duplicate or missing `id` makes a park unreachable | low | `parks.find` returns the first; sample ids are unique and `id` is required per `park-data.md` | reject (unlikely) |
| EC4/BH9 | Header shows "1 parks" / "0 parks" | low | Sample always has 12; pluralization and an empty state add branches | reject (unlikely) |
| EC5 | Array entry passes `typeof === 'object'` and becomes a phantom park | low | True, but the data never contains nested arrays; the fix adds a guard | reject (unlikely) |
| EC6/BH4 | Stem-only image mapping would replace a real image with the same filename | low | Stem mapping is exactly what `park-data.md` specifies; only hypothetical future URLs hit it | reject (unlikely, by design) |
| EC7/BH1 | No AbortController or timeout; a hung fetch shows loading forever, StrictMode double-fetches | low | The `cancelled` flag prevents stale state; a same-origin static file is unlikely to hang; a timeout adds complexity | reject (unlikely) |
| EC8 | Sort test oracle uses a different `localeCompare` than production | low | No case or accent ties among sample names, so both orders agree | reject (unlikely) |
| BH2 | Focus is lost when Retry unmounts | low | Real, but only on the rare error path; focus management adds code; Story 1.5 does the a11y pass | reject (unlikely) |
| BH3 | `hasCoords` accepts out-of-range lat/lng | low | `finiteNumber` only; `lat: 200` gives `hasCoords: true`, which breaks the Story 1.4 markers; "valid lat/lng" in CAP-1 implies range. Direct correction of an existing guard | patch |
| BH5 | `text()` tests the trimmed value but returns the untrimmed string | low | Padded names would sort and number differently; the fix is a direct correction (return the trimmed value) | patch |
| BH6 | No tie-breaker in `byName` | low | No duplicate names in the sample; adding a sort key is extra logic | reject (unlikely) |
| BH10 | Normalization rules and the non-array load path are untested | — | Duplicate of VG1–VG3 | merged into VG |
| BH11 | Byte-identical AC is not automated | false | The spec's Verification uses `cmp`; it ran and passed | reject |
| BH12 | Load error reason is swallowed | low | Diagnostic only; the error UI works | reject (unlikely) |
| BH13 | No `engines` field makes install non-reproducible | false | `package-lock.json` pins versions; vitest 4 and jsdom 29 install cleanly on current Node lines | reject |
| BH14 | `document.title` is not updated per view; favicon 404 | low | Cosmetic; per-view titles fit the Story 1.5 a11y pass | reject (unlikely) |
| BH15 | No `<h1>` during loading or error | low | Transient state; a11y pass in 1.5 | reject (unlikely) |
| VG1 | Null-name ordering in `byName` is untested | medium | Pre-verified: removing the null guards passes all tests, but a nameless record would crash the load | patch |
| VG2 | Non-array payload rejection is untested | medium | Pre-verified: deleting the `Array.isArray` check makes corrupt data look like an empty set, and no test fails | patch |
| VG3 | Whitespace-to-null, `stringList` filtering and negative `acreage` are untested | medium | Pre-verified: simplifying `text()` or dropping `acreage >= 0` passes all tests | patch |
| VG-o | Image-mapping tests assert `.png`, but the build inlines data URIs | false | The tests run in vitest, where `?url` yields file URLs; mapping by stem is what is being tested, and the build passes | reject |

## Design Notes

The park `number` is assigned once at load time and shared by list cards and map markers (Stories 1.2 and 1.4), so it must not be recomputed per view. `rating` stays `null` when it is not a finite number in 0–5, so the UI can never show 0 stars by accident.

- `npm test` -- expected: all tests pass.
- `npm run build` -- expected: succeeds with no errors.
- `cmp requirements/parks.sample.json public/data/parks.sample.json` -- expected: no output.

**Manual checks (if no CLI):**
- `npm run dev`, then open `/` and `/parks/nope`: the landmarks are present, "Park not found" shows with its link, and Tab shows the amber focus ring on the link.

### Story 1.3: Park details with missing-data handling

As a resident,
I want to see everything known about a park, and a clear note where information is missing,
So that I can decide whether to visit without being misled.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** the user opens `/parks/:id`
**When** details render
**Then** an "All parks" back control appears first, then the photo area, the park name as the view's single `<h1>`, the address, an "Open in OpenStreetMap" link from lat/lng, fact tiles in a `<dl>` for Hours, Size (acres), and Rating ("4.7 / 5"), the About description, and all amenities as checked tags
**And** focus moves to the details heading when details open

**Given** a park with missing data
**When** details render
**Then** each case follows the `design/README.md` missing-data table:
- missing `location.address` (Highland Dog Park) → "Address not listed"
- `description: null` (Old Mill Botanical Garden) → "No description has been provided for this park."
- `rating: null` (Cedar Hill) → "Not yet rated", never 0 or empty stars
- `images: []` (Cedar Hill, East Ridge) → a dashed "No photos for this park yet" box
- missing `hours` or `acreage` → "Not listed"
- missing or empty `amenities` → the section is hidden or shows "No amenities listed"
- missing lat/lng → no OpenStreetMap link
**And** missing-value fact tiles use the muted style (missing background, dashed border, italic text)

**Given** an image fails to load
**When** the `<img>` fires `onError`
**Then** the no-photo box replaces it, with no broken-image icon

**Given** a park has images
**When** details render
**Then** the first image shows with a "Photo 1 of N" label and alt text naming the park

**Given** amenity tokens such as `dog-run`
**When** they render
**Then** they display as readable labels ("Dog run"), and unknown tokens are humanized the same way, never dropped

import { useCallback, useEffect, useState } from 'react'
import { Link, Route, Routes, useParams } from 'react-router'
import ParkList from './components/ParkList.jsx'
import { useParks } from './data/useParks.js'

const cardClass = 'rounded-[10px] border border-line bg-surface p-4'
const linkClass = 'focus-ring rounded text-forest underline'

function NotFound() {
  return (
    <div>
      <h1 className="text-lg font-bold">Park not found</h1>
      <p className="mt-2">
        <Link to="/" className={linkClass}>
          Back to all parks
        </Link>
      </p>
    </div>
  )
}

function ParkDetailsPlaceholder({ parks, onOpen }) {
  const { id } = useParams()
  const park = parks.find((p) => p.id === id)

  // Record the opened park so the list can return focus to its card.
  useEffect(() => {
    if (park) onOpen(park.id)
  }, [park, onOpen])

  if (!park) return <NotFound />

  return <h1 className="text-[28px] font-extrabold">{park.name}</h1>
}

function ParksPanel({ status, parks, retry, lastParkId, setLastParkId }) {
  const clearLastParkId = useCallback(() => setLastParkId(null), [setLastParkId])

  if (status === 'loading') {
    return (
      <p role="status" className={cardClass}>
        Loading parks…
      </p>
    )
  }

  if (status === 'error') {
    return (
      <div role="alert" className={cardClass}>
        <p>Couldn't load parks. Try again.</p>
        <button
          type="button"
          onClick={retry}
          className="focus-ring mt-3 min-h-11 rounded-lg bg-forest px-4 font-semibold text-white hover:bg-forest-dark"
        >
          Retry
        </button>
      </div>
    )
  }

  return (
    <Routes>
      <Route
        path="/"
        element={
          <ParkList parks={parks} focusParkId={lastParkId} onFocusUsed={clearLastParkId} />
        }
      />
      <Route
        path="/parks/:id"
        element={<ParkDetailsPlaceholder parks={parks} onOpen={setLastParkId} />}
      />
      <Route path="*" element={<NotFound />} />
    </Routes>
  )
}

export default function App() {
  const { status, parks, retry } = useParks()
  // Id of the last-opened park. Lives in App state (not location.state) so
  // focus can return to its card after browser Back, which carries no state.
  const [lastParkId, setLastParkId] = useState(null)

  return (
    <div className="flex min-h-screen flex-col">
      <header className="bg-forest px-5 py-4 text-white">
        <p className="text-xl font-extrabold">Find a Park</p>
        <p className="text-sm">New York City area</p>
        {status === 'ready' && <p className="text-sm">{parks.length} parks</p>}
      </header>
      <main className="flex flex-1 flex-col">
        <aside aria-label="Parks" className="p-5">
          <ParksPanel
            status={status}
            parks={parks}
            retry={retry}
            lastParkId={lastParkId}
            setLastParkId={setLastParkId}
          />
        </aside>
        <section aria-label="Map of parks" className="flex-1 p-5">
          <p className="text-muted">Map coming soon.</p>
        </section>
      </main>
    </div>
  )
}
/** Human label for a kebab-case amenity token: `dog-run` → "Dog run". Unknown tokens are humanized, never dropped. */
export function amenityLabel(token) {
  const spaced = String(token ?? '').trim().replace(/-/g, ' ')
  return spaced.charAt(0).toUpperCase() + spaced.slice(1)
}

// Null-safe park data layer. Normalization never invents values and never
// mutates the parsed source: missing scalars become `null`, missing arrays `[]`.

const imageModules = import.meta.glob('../assets/park-images/*.png', {
  eager: true,
  query: '?url',
  import: 'default',
})

// filename stem (e.g. "prospect-1") -> bundled asset URL
const bundledImages = Object.fromEntries(
  Object.entries(imageModules).map(([path, url]) => [fileStem(path), url]),
)

function fileStem(pathOrUrl) {
  const withoutQuery = pathOrUrl.split(/[?#]/)[0]
  const file = withoutQuery.split('/').pop() ?? ''
  const dot = file.lastIndexOf('.')
  return dot > 0 ? file.slice(0, dot) : file
}

/** Map a source image URL to the bundled art with the same filename stem; unmatched URLs pass through unchanged. */
export function resolveImageUrl(url) {
  if (typeof url !== 'string') return url
  return bundledImages[fileStem(url)] ?? url
}

function text(value) {
  if (typeof value !== 'string') return null
  const trimmed = value.trim()
  return trimmed !== '' ? trimmed : null
}

function finiteNumber(value) {
  return typeof value === 'number' && Number.isFinite(value) ? value : null
}

function numberInRange(value, min, max) {
  const n = finiteNumber(value)
  return n !== null && n >= min && n <= max ? n : null
}

function stringList(value) {
  return Array.isArray(value) ? value.filter((item) => text(item) !== null) : []
}

export function normalizePark(raw) {
  const park = raw && typeof raw === 'object' ? raw : {}
  const location = park.location && typeof park.location === 'object' ? park.location : {}
  const lat = numberInRange(location.lat, -90, 90)
  const lng = numberInRange(location.lng, -180, 180)
  const acreage = finiteNumber(park.acreage)
  const rating = finiteNumber(park.rating)

  return {
    id: text(park.id),
    name: text(park.name),
    number: null,
    description: text(park.description),
    address: text(location.address),
    lat,
    lng,
    hasCoords: lat !== null && lng !== null,
    amenities: stringList(park.amenities),
    hours: text(park.hours),
    images: stringList(park.images).map(resolveImageUrl),
    acreage: acreage !== null && acreage >= 0 ? acreage : null,
    rating: rating !== null && rating >= 0 && rating <= 5 ? rating : null,
  }
}

function byName(a, b) {
  if (a.name === null) return b.name === null ? 0 : 1
  if (b.name === null) return -1
  return a.name.localeCompare(b.name, 'en', { sensitivity: 'base' })
}

/** Normalize, sort A–Z by name and number 1–N. The number is assigned once here and shared by every view. */
export function normalizeParks(rawParks) {
  if (!Array.isArray(rawParks)) return []

# Find a Park: design handoff

UI designs for the Find a Park take-home. They were drawn on a Claude design canvas before any code was written, and they are the visual spec for the React + Tailwind + Leaflet build. The full AI transcript of the design session is in the repo alongside this folder.

**These are reference designs, not code.** Build with React, Tailwind and react-leaflet (no component library), and use the screenshots plus the rules below. Where the brief and these designs disagree, the brief wins.

## Contents

| File | What it shows |
|---|---|
| `screens/01-desktop-list-map.png` | Desktop default: park list on the left, map on the right |
| `screens/02-desktop-park-selected.png` | Desktop with a park selected: details replace the list, the marker is highlighted |
| `screens/03-desktop-missing-data.png` | Desktop details for a park with missing data (Cedar Hill: no photos, no rating) |
| `screens/04-phone-list.png` | Phone, List tab |
| `screens/05-phone-map-selected.png` | Phone, Map tab, with a park selected (preview card) |
| `screens/06-phone-details.png` | Phone details, full data (Prospect Park) |
| `screens/07-phone-missing-data.png` | Phone details, missing data (Cedar Hill) |
| `source/*.dc.html` | Canvas source files, for reference only. They need the canvas runtime and are **not** meant to run in the app. |

Screenshots are at 2x: desktop at 1280×800, phone at 390×844. The map in every screenshot is a drawn stand-in. The real app uses Leaflet with OpenStreetMap tiles.

## Layout and behaviour

### Desktop (≥ 768px, Tailwind `md:`)

- A green header with the app name, a subtitle and the park count.
- Two columns:
  - **Left sidebar (~400px):** scrolls on its own.
  - **Map:** fills the rest of the screen.
- **Sidebar, list mode:** an "All parks" heading, a one-line hint, then one card per park.
- **Selecting a park** from either the list or the map:
  - The sidebar switches to details mode, starting with an "All parks" back button.
  - That park's marker turns amber and gets bigger.
  - The URL should change too (e.g. `?park=prospect-park` or `/parks/:id`), so details can be linked and the browser Back button works.
- **Going back to the list:** move focus to the card of the park you came from.
- **Opening details:** move focus to the details heading, so keyboard and screen-reader users land in the right place.

### Phone (< 768px)

- One column. A **List / Map** toggle sits under the header, made of two real `<button>`s with `aria-pressed`. List is the default.
- **Tapping a marker** opens a preview card at the bottom of the map. It shows the park's position in the list ("Park 8 of 12"), its name, address and hours, a **View park details** button and a close button.
- **Details** take the whole screen, with an "All parks" back link in the header.

### List ↔ map link

- Parks are sorted A–Z and numbered 1–12. The same number appears on the list card and on the map marker, so the two views always match.
- Every marker is focusable and named "8. Prospect Park" for screen readers. Use `L.divIcon` with a `<button>` inside it, or set `keyboard: true` and `alt`/`title` on the markers.
- The list is the main way to choose a park. The map is never the only way.

## Park card (list)

- Number badge, park name (17px bold), address, and the first three amenities as tags. If there are more, add a "+N more" tag.
- The whole card is a single `<button>` (or a link once there is routing), so it gets keyboard focus and one Tab stop.

## Details

The details view shows, from top to bottom:

1. Photo area (see the missing-data rules below)
2. Park name as the page heading (26–28px, 800 weight), then the address
3. An **Open in OpenStreetMap** link built from the park's lat/lng (`https://www.openstreetmap.org/?mlat={lat}&mlon={lng}#map=16/{lat}/{lng}`). This was added during design and is easy to drop if you don't want it.
4. Three fact tiles: **Hours**, **Size** (acres), **Rating** ("4.7 / 5")
5. **About** (the description)
6. **Amenities**: all of them, as tags with a check mark. Turn the raw values into labels by replacing `-` with a space and capitalising the first letter (`dog-run` → "Dog run").

## Missing data: never invent a value

The brief requires this, and the sample data has every one of these cases.

| Field | When missing / null / empty | Show |
|---|---|---|
| `location.address` | Highland Dog Park | "Address not listed" in the secondary text colour |
| `description` | Old Mill Botanical Garden (`null`) | "No description has been provided for this park." |
| `rating` | Cedar Hill (`null`) | "Not yet rated" (do not show 0 or blank stars) |
| `images` | Cedar Hill, East Ridge (`[]`) | A dashed box: image icon + "No photos for this park yet" |
| `images` failing to load | **Every** sample URL (`images.example.com` doesn't exist) | The same fallback, triggered by `onError` on `<img>`; never a broken-image icon |
| `hours` | not in the sample, but handle it | "Not listed" |
| `acreage` | not in the sample, but handle it | "Not listed" |
| `amenities` | not in the sample, but handle it | Hide the Amenities section, or show "No amenities listed" |
| `location.lat/lng` | not in the sample, but handle it | Keep the park in the list, leave it off the map, and hide the map link |

Fact tiles with a missing value use the muted look from screen 07: `#F0EDE5` background, dashed `#C9C4B6` border, italic text. *Remark: the desktop screenshot (03) shows "Not yet rated" with the normal tile style. That is a mistake in the mock, and the phone version is the intended look.*

Wrap every field in a small helper (e.g. `display(value, fallback)`) so the rules stay in one place. Never let a missing nested field crash the app (`park.location?.address`).

## Design tokens (Tailwind)

Add these under `theme.extend` (or as CSS variables with Tailwind v4 `@theme`):

```js
colors: {
  paper:   '#F6F4EE', // page background
  surface: '#FFFFFF', // cards, tiles
  line:    '#DAD6CB', // card borders
  ink:     '#1B2620', // main text
  muted:   '#4F5B54', // secondary text (≈7:1 on white)
  forest:  { DEFAULT: '#1E5B3F', dark: '#123826' }, // header, primary buttons, markers, links
  tag:     { bg: '#E9EFE7', fg: '#23472F' },         // amenity tags
  amber:   '#B45309', // selected marker and focus ring
  missing: { bg: '#F0EDE5', line: '#C9C4B6' },       // missing-value tiles, no-photo box
  map:     { land: '#E4E8DF', water: '#B9D3DC' },    // only for the drawn map; real tiles replace these
},
fontFamily: { sans: ['"Public Sans"', '"Segoe UI"', 'system-ui', 'sans-serif'] },
```

- **Font:** Public Sans from Google Fonts, weights 400/500/600/700/800.
- **Type sizes:**
  - Body 15–16px; card titles 17px bold
  - Details heading 26–28px (800 weight); section headings 17–18px
  - Small labels 13px; nothing smaller except the map attribution
- **Corner radius:** cards 10px, buttons 8–10px, tags fully rounded.
- **Spacing:** sidebar padding 20px (16px on phone), 10px between cards, 14–16px inside cards.

## Accessibility

These checks match what the brief evaluates:

- **Focus ring:** every interactive element gets a visible ring, `focus-visible:outline focus-visible:outline-[3px] focus-visible:outline-amber focus-visible:outline-offset-2`. Never remove outlines without putting this ring in their place.
- **Touch targets:** at least 44×44px, including the zoom buttons, the close button and the back button.
- **Real elements:** use `<button>`, `<a href>`, `<ul>/<li>` for the list, `<dl>/<dt>/<dd>` for the fact tiles, and one `<h1>` per view. No clickable `<div>`s.
- **Landmarks:** `<header>`, `<main>`, an `<aside aria-label="Parks">` for the sidebar, and a map section labelled "Map of parks".
- **Labels:** icon-only buttons need an `aria-label` ("Zoom in", "Close park preview").
- **Contrast:** text has at least 4.5:1 contrast. The colours above meet that, including white on forest.
- **Location:** never ask for the user's location; the app must work without it. Centre the map on a fixed view, or fit it to the parks' bounds.

## Remarks and open decisions

- **Municipality:** the parks span Brooklyn, Manhattan, Queens and the Bronx and are mostly fictional (Prospect Park is real). The designs call the area "New York City area". Say so in the project README.
- **Cedar Hill coordinates** (40.7128, -74.006) are exactly the standard "New York City" point, so they are probably a placeholder. The park is shown as given; mention it in Known issues.
- **Short addresses** such as "Ridge Rd" and "Shore Pkwy" are shown as given, without adding a city.
- **Not designed:**
  - Search and filters (optional in the brief)
  - Current-location support (optional)
  - A loading state and an error state for when `parks.json` fails to load. Both are worth adding: a short "Loading parks…" message and a "Couldn't load parks. Try again." message with a retry button, using the same card style.
- **Hours** are shown exactly as written in the data ("Dawn to dusk", "24 hours"). An "Open now" badge was left out on purpose, because parsing these strings reliably is out of scope.
- **Photos:** there is no gallery. The mock shows the first photo with a "Photo 1 of N" label. Showing a single image, or a simple row of thumbnails, is fine.
- **Map tiles:** OpenStreetMap's tile server needs no API key; keep its attribution visible. For real public use, switch to a tile provider that allows production traffic. That belongs in the README's next steps.
import { Fragment, useEffect, useRef } from 'react'
import { Link } from 'react-router'
import { amenityLabel } from '../data/format.js'

const MAX_TAGS = 3

function ParkCard({ park, linkRef }) {
  const shown = park.amenities.slice(0, MAX_TAGS)
  const extra = park.amenities.length - shown.length
  const tags = shown.map(amenityLabel)
  if (extra > 0) tags.push(`+${extra} more`)

  return (
    <li>
      <Link
        ref={linkRef}
        to={`/parks/${park.id}`}
        className="focus-ring flex min-h-11 items-start gap-3 rounded-[10px] border border-line bg-surface p-4 hover:border-forest"
      >
        <span
          aria-hidden="true"
          className="flex size-8 shrink-0 items-center justify-center rounded-full bg-forest text-sm font-bold text-white"
        >
          {park.number}
        </span>
        <span className="sr-only">{park.number}.</span>{' '}
        <span className="min-w-0 flex-1">
          <span className="block text-[17px] font-bold">{park.name}</span>{' '}
          {park.address ? (
            <span className="block text-muted">{park.address}</span>
          ) : (
            <span className="block text-muted">Address not listed</span>
          )}{' '}
          {tags.length > 0 && (
            <span data-testid="park-tags" className="mt-2 flex flex-wrap gap-2">
              {tags.map((label, index) => (
                <Fragment key={`${index}-${label}`}>
                  <span className="rounded-full bg-tag-bg px-2.5 py-0.5 text-sm text-tag-fg">
                    {label}
                  </span>{' '}
                </Fragment>
              ))}
            </span>
          )}
        </span>
        <svg
          aria-hidden="true"
          viewBox="0 0 24 24"
          className="size-5 shrink-0 self-center text-muted"
          fill="none"
          stroke="currentColor"
          strokeWidth="2"
        >
          <path d="m9 6 6 6-6 6" />
        </svg>
      </Link>
    </li>
  )
}

/**
 * The "All parks" list. Parks are rendered in loader order with loader numbers.
 * If `focusParkId` matches a card on mount, that card's link gets focus and
 * `onFocusUsed` is called so a later fresh visit does not steal focus.
 */
export default function ParkList({ parks, focusParkId, onFocusUsed }) {
  const linkRefs = useRef(new Map())

  useEffect(() => {
    if (!focusParkId) return
    linkRefs.current.get(focusParkId)?.focus()
    onFocusUsed?.()
    // Run once on mount: the id is read once, then cleared by the parent.
  }, [])

  return (
    <div>
      <h1 className="text-lg font-bold">All parks</h1>
      <p className="mt-1 text-muted">Choose a park to see hours, amenities and directions.</p>
      <ul className="mt-4 flex flex-col gap-3">
        {parks.map((park) => (
          <ParkCard
            key={park.id}
            park={park}
            linkRef={(node) => {
              if (node) linkRefs.current.set(park.id, node)
              else linkRefs.current.delete(park.id)
            }}
          />
        ))}
      </ul>
    </div>
  )
}

const sample = JSON.parse(

const ERROR_TEXT = "Couldn't load parks. Try again."

function renderAt(path) {

    <MemoryRouter initialEntries={[path]}>

  )

afterEach(() => vi.unstubAllGlobals())

describe('App shell', () => {
  it('goes loading → error → retry → ready', async () => {
    let rejectFirst
    const fetchMock = vi
      .fn()
      .mockImplementationOnce(
        () =>
          new Promise((_resolve, reject) => {
            rejectFirst = reject
          }),
      )
      .mockResolvedValueOnce(okResponse())
    vi.stubGlobal('fetch', fetchMock)
    const user = userEvent.setup()

    renderAt('/')
    const aside = screen.getByRole('complementary', { name: 'Parks' })
    expect(within(aside).getByText('Loading parks…')).toBeInTheDocument()

    rejectFirst(new TypeError('network down'))
    expect(await within(aside).findByText(ERROR_TEXT)).toBeInTheDocument()

    await user.click(within(aside).getByRole('button', { name: 'Retry' }))
    expect(await within(aside).findByRole('heading', { name: 'All parks' })).toBeInTheDocument()
    expect(screen.getByText('12 parks')).toBeInTheDocument()
    expect(fetchMock).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(2)
  })

  it('shows the error state for a non-OK response', async () => {
    vi.stubGlobal('fetch', vi.fn().mockResolvedValue({ ok: false, status: 404 }))
    renderAt('/')
    expect(await screen.findByText(ERROR_TEXT)).toBeInTheDocument()
    expect(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Retry' })).toBeInTheDocument()
  })

  "content": "---\ntitle: 'Story 1.3: Park details with missing-data handling'\ntype: 'feature'\ncreated: '2026-10-14'\nstatus: 'draft'\nroute: 'dispatch'\nreview_loop_iteration: 0\ncontext:\n  - '{project-root}/_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/epic-1-context.md'\n---\n\n<frozen-after-approval reason=\"human-owned intent — do not modify unless human renegotiates\">\n\n## Intent\n\n**Problem:** `/parks/:id` shows only the park name (`ParkDetailsPlaceholder`). Residents can't see hours, size, rating, description, photos or amenities, or tell when a value is missing rather than wrong (CAP-3).\n\n**Approach:** Replace the placeholder with a `ParkDetails` view in this order: an \"All parks\" back link, the photo area, the name as `<h1>`, the address, an \"Open in OpenStreetMap\" link, a `<dl>` of Hours/Size/Rating tiles, About, and Amenities. Every missing value gets the labelled fallback from `design/README.md`. Focus moves to the `<h1>` when details open.\n\n## Boundaries & Constraints\n\n**Always:** render the normalized park exactly as given and never invent a value; fallback copy matches the design table word for word; missing-value tiles use `bg-missing-bg`, a dashed `border-missing-line` and italic text; `focus-ring` and ≥ 44px targets on the back link and the OSM link; reuse `amenityLabel` from `src/data/format.js`; keep the `NotFound` branch and the `onOpen` id recording.\n\n**Never:** a photo gallery or carousel (first photo only); map, markers or marker highlight (1.4); the desktop sidebar/phone header layout split or the List/Map toggle (1.5); an \"Open now\" badge or hours parsing; star icons; clickable `<div>`s; editing `parks.sample.json` or the normalizer.\n\n**Decisions (agent-made, user-invisible or design-permitted):** empty amenities shows the Amenities heading with \"No amenities listed\" rather than hiding it. Rating shows one decimal (`4` → \"4.0 / 5\"). Size shows \"N acres\" (\"1 acre\" when N is 1). Photo alt is \"Photo of {name}\". The back control is a `<Link to=\"/\">`, so the existing `lastParkId` mechanism returns focus to the card.\n\n## I/O & Edge-Case Matrix\n\n| Scenario | Input / State | Expected Output / Behavior | Error Handling |\n|----------|--------------|---------------------------|----------------|\n| Full data | Prospect Park | all sections; \"Photo 1 of 2\"; tiles \"6:00 AM - 1:00 AM\", \"526 acres\", \"4.7 / 5\"; 7 checked amenity tags | N/A |\n| Missing address | Highland Dog Park | \"Address not listed\" in `text-muted` | N/A |\n| Null description | Old Mill Botanical Garden | About reads \"No description has been provided for this park.\" | N/A |\n| Null rating | Cedar Hill | Rating tile \"Not yet rated\", muted style; no \"0\" | N/A |\n| No images | Cedar Hill, East Ridge | dashed \"No photos for this park yet\" box, no `<img>` | N/A |\n| Image fails | `<img>` fires `error` | the no-photo box replaces it | no broken-image icon |\n| Missing hours / acreage | synthetic park | the tile reads \"Not listed\", muted style | N/A |\n| Empty amenities | synthetic `amenities: []` | \"No amenities listed\" | N/A |\n| No coordinates | synthetic `hasCoords: false` | no OSM link | N/A |\n| Unknown amenity | `wifi`, `dog-run` | \"Wifi\", \"Dog run\" | humanized, never dropped |\n| Back control | click \"All parks\" | `/` renders; that park's card has focus | N/A |\n\n</frozen-after-approval>\n\n## Code Map\n\n- `src/App.jsx` -- `ParkDetailsPlaceholder` (find park by `:id`, call `onOpen`, `NotFound` fallback) is the code to replace. Move that lookup into the new component, or wrap it. `linkClass`/`cardClass` are reusable styles. `lastParkId` + `ParkList focusParkId` already restore card focus on any return to `/`.\n- `src/data/parks.js` -- normalized park `{ id, name, number, description, address, lat, lng, hasCoords, amenities, hours, images, acreage, rating }`. Missing values are `null`/`[]`, `rating` is in 0–5 or `null`, and `images` already resolve to bundled assets. Don't change it.\n- `src/data/format.js` -- `amenityLabel`; add the small display helpers here (the epic asks for a central `display(value, fallback)`-style rule set).\n- `src/index.css` -- tokens `missing-bg`, `missing-line`, `tag-bg`, `tag-fg`, `muted`, `forest`, `line`, `surface`; the `focus-ring` utility.\n- `src/App.test.jsx` -- the `renderAt(path)`, `okResponse()` (structuredClone of the sample, so tests can mutate it for synthetic parks) and `TestNav` patterns.\n- `design/screens/02-desktop-park-selected.png`, `06-phone-details.png`, `07-phone-missing-data.png` -- the visuals: bordered \"‹ All parks\" button, a rounded photo with a dark \"Photo 1 of N\" pill at bottom-right, a 3-column tile grid, ✓ tags.\n- OSM URL: `https://www.openstreetmap.org/?mlat={lat}&mlon={lng}#map=16/{lat}/{lng}`.\n\n## Tasks & Acceptance\n\n**Execution:**\n- [ ] `src/data/format.js` -- add `formatRating(n)` → \"4.7 / 5\" or `null`, `formatAcres(n)` → \"526 acres\" / \"1 acre\" or `null`, `osmUrl(lat, lng)`; one fallback rule per field lives with the view's `display(value, fallback)` usage.\n- [ ] `src/components/ParkDetails.jsx` -- `ParkDetails({ park })` renders the sections in order: `PhotoArea` (keyed by park id, `onError` → no-photo box), an `<h1 tabIndex={-1}>` focused on mount, a `<dl>` of `FactTile`s (muted variant when the value is missing), About, and Amenities as a `<ul>` of ✓ tags.\n- [ ] `src/App.jsx` -- the `/parks/:id` route renders `ParkDetails` for a found park (keep `onOpen` and `NotFound`); delete `ParkDetailsPlaceholder`.\n- [ ] `src/data/format.test.js`, `src/App.test.jsx` -- unit-test the new helpers; test every matrix row (synthetic rows by editing the cloned sample in a custom fetch mock), section order, and focus on the heading after opening from the list.\n\n**Acceptance Criteria:**\n- Given the list, when the user opens a park by keyboard, then the details `<h1>` has focus and is the only `<h1>` in the view.\n- Given details, when the user activates \"All parks\", then the list renders and focus is on that park's card.\n- Given a park with images, then one `<img>` shows with alt naming the park and a \"Photo 1 of N\" label.\n- Given any of the 12 sample parks, when details render, then nothing crashes and no value reads \"null\", \"undefined\" or \"NaN\".\n\n## Implementation Notes\n\n## Spec Change Log\n\n## Review Triage Log\n\n## Design Notes\n\nFocus-to-heading uses a ref plus `useEffect` keyed on `park.id`, so moving from one park to another also refocuses. The heading needs `tabIndex={-1}` to be focusable from code, and it gets no `focus-ring`, since a programmatic focus target is not a Tab stop. `PhotoArea` keeps a `failed` state and is keyed by park id, so a failure on one park does not hide another park's photo.\n\nMissing-value tile golden example:\n\n```jsx\n<div className={missing ? 'border-dashed border-missing-line bg-missing-bg' : 'border-line bg-surface'}>\n  <dt className=\"text-sm text-muted\">Rating</dt>\n  <dd className={missing ? 'italic text-muted' : 'font-semibold'}>{value ?? fallback}</dd>\n</div>\n```\n\n## Verification\n\n**Commands:**\n- `npm test` -- expected: all tests pass.\n- `npm run build` -- expected: succeeds with no errors.\n\n**Manual checks (if no CLI):**\n- `npm run dev`. Open Cedar Hill: dashed photo box and a muted \"Not yet rated\" tile. Open Prospect Park: the photo shows \"Photo 1 of 2\". Tab to \"All parks\", press Enter, and focus lands on the card.\n"

===== 2026-10-15T05:45:09.490000+00:00  [main]  assistant =====
I wrote the spec for story 1.3: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-1-3-park-details-with-missing-data-handling.md`

**What 1.3 adds:**
- A new `ParkDetails` component replaces the placeholder at `/parks/:id`.
- The details read top to bottom: an "All parks" back link, the first photo labelled "Photo 1 of N", the name as the page heading, the address, an "Open in OpenStreetMap" link, Hours/Size/Rating fact tiles, About, and amenities as ✓ tags.
- Missing values show the wording from the design notes, word for word. Missing fact tiles get the dashed, italic muted style. A photo that fails to load is replaced by the "No photos for this park yet" box.
- Keyboard focus moves to the park heading when details open. The "All parks" link reuses the focus-return from story 1.2, so focus lands back on the park's card in the list.
- New helpers go in `src/data/format.js`: `display`, `formatRating`, `formatAcres` and `osmUrl`.
- Tests cover every row of the edge-case table, including made-up parks with no hours, acreage, amenities or coordinates.

**Choices I made where the design allows either, or you wouldn't notice:**
- A park with no amenities shows "No amenities listed" instead of hiding the section.
- Ratings always show one decimal ("4.0 / 5").
- Size reads "1 acre" for a single acre.
- Photo alt text is "Photo of {name}".

**Size:** the spec is about 1,800 tokens, a bit over the 1,600 guideline. It's one goal, so splitting it doesn't make sense, and most of the extra comes from the edge-case table. I recommend keeping it as is.

**Unfinished in 1.2:** story 1-2 is still `in-progress`, and its 3 review patches haven't been applied: one test assertion, the `amenityLabel` dash/trim order, and an address ternary. 1.3 doesn't depend on them. The `amenityLabel` fix touches `format.js`, which 1.3 also edits, so it's worth doing soon.

---

Before approving, you can open the spec file in an editor or ask me questions and tell me what to change. You can also use `bmad-advanced-elicitation` or `bmad-party-mode`, ideally in another session to avoid context bloat.

13	<frozen-after-approval reason="human-owned intent — do not modify unless human renegotiates">
14	
15	## Intent
16	
17	**Problem:** `/parks/:id` shows only the park name (`ParkDetailsPlaceholder`). Residents can't see hours, size, rating, description, photos or amenities, or tell when a value is missing rather than wrong (CAP-3).
18	
19	**Approach:** Replace the placeholder with a `ParkDetails` view in this order: an "All parks" back link, the photo area, the name as `<h1>`, the address, an "Open in OpenStreetMap" link, a `<dl>` of Hours/Size/Rating tiles, About, and Amenities. Every missing value gets the labelled fallback from `design/README.md`. Focus moves to the `<h1>` when details open.
20	
21	## Boundaries & Constraints
22	
23	**Always:** render the normalized park exactly as given and never invent a value; fallback copy matches the design table word for word; missing-value tiles use `bg-missing-bg`, a dashed `border-missing-line` and italic text; `focus-ring` and ≥ 44px targets on the back link and the OSM link; reuse `amenityLabel` from `src/data/format.js`; keep the `NotFound` branch and the `onOpen` id recording.
24	
25	**Never:** a photo gallery or carousel (first photo only); map, markers or marker highlight (1.4); the desktop sidebar/phone header layout split or the List/Map toggle (1.5); an "Open now" badge or hours parsing; star icons; clickable `<div>`s; editing `parks.sample.json` or the normalizer.
26	
27	**Decisions (agent-made, user-invisible or design-permitted):** empty amenities shows the Amenities heading with "No amenities listed" rather than hiding it. Rating shows one decimal (`4` → "4.0 / 5"). Size shows "N acres" ("1 acre" when N is 1). Photo alt is "Photo of {name}". The back control is a `<Link to="/">`, so the existing `lastParkId` mechanism returns focus to the card.
28	
29	## I/O & Edge-Case Matrix
30	
31	| Scenario | Input / State | Expected Output / Behavior | Error Handling |
32	|----------|--------------|---------------------------|----------------|
33	| Full data | Prospect Park | all sections; "Photo 1 of 2"; tiles "6:00 AM - 1:00 AM", "526 acres", "4.7 / 5"; 7 checked amenity tags | N/A |
34	| Missing address | Highland Dog Park | "Address not listed" in `text-muted` | N/A |
35	| Null description | Old Mill Botanical Garden | About reads "No description has been provided for this park." | N/A |
36	| Null rating | Cedar Hill | Rating tile "Not yet rated", muted style; no "0" | N/A |
37	| No images | Cedar Hill, East Ridge | dashed "No photos for this park yet" box, no `<img>` | N/A |
38	| Image fails | `<img>` fires `error` | the no-photo box replaces it | no broken-image icon |
39	| Missing hours / acreage | synthetic park | the tile reads "Not listed", muted style | N/A |
40	| Empty amenities | synthetic `amenities: []` | "No amenities listed" | N/A |
41	| No coordinates | synthetic `hasCoords: false` | no OSM link | N/A |
42	| Unknown amenity | `wifi`, `dog-run` | "Wifi", "Dog run" | humanized, never dropped |
43	| Back control | click "All parks" | `/` renders; that park's card has focus | N/A |
44	
45	</frozen-after-approval>
46	
47	## Code Map
48	
49	- `src/App.jsx` -- `ParkDetailsPlaceholder` (find park by `:id`, call `onOpen`, `NotFound` fallback) is the code to replace. Move that lookup into the new component, or wrap it. `linkClass`/`cardClass` are reusable styles. `lastParkId` + `ParkList focusParkId` already restore card focus on any return to `/`.
50	- `src/data/parks.js` -- normalized park `{ id, name, number, description, address, lat, lng, hasCoords, amenities, hours, images, acreage, rating }`. Missing values are `null`/`[]`, `rating` is in 0–5 or `null`, and `images` already resolve to bundled assets. Don't change it.
51	- `src/data/format.js` -- `amenityLabel`; add the small display helpers here (the epic asks for a central `display(value, fallback)`-style rule set).
52	- `src/index.css` -- tokens `missing-bg`, `missing-line`, `tag-bg`, `tag-fg`, `muted`, `forest`, `line`, `surface`; the `focus-ring` utility.
53	- `src/App.test.jsx` -- the `renderAt(path)`, `okResponse()` (structuredClone of the sample, so tests can mutate it for synthetic parks) and `TestNav` patterns.
54	- `design/screens/02-desktop-park-selected.png`, `06-phone-details.png`, `07-phone-missing-data.png` -- the visuals: bordered "‹ All parks" button, a rounded photo with a dark "Photo 1 of N" pill at bottom-right, a 3-column tile grid, ✓ tags.
55	- OSM URL: `https://www.openstreetmap.org/?mlat={lat}&mlon={lng}#map=16/{lat}/{lng}`.
56	
57	## Tasks & Acceptance
58	
59	**Execution:**
60	- [ ] `src/data/format.js` -- add `formatRating(n)` → "4.7 / 5" or `null`, `formatAcres(n)` → "526 acres" / "1 acre" or `null`, `osmUrl(lat, lng)`, and `display(value, fallback)` → `{ text, missing }` so the view applies every fallback through one rule.
61	- [ ] `src/components/ParkDetails.jsx` -- `ParkDetails({ park })` renders the sections in order: `PhotoArea` (keyed by park id, `onError` → no-photo box), an `<h1 tabIndex={-1}>` focused on mount, a `<dl>` of `FactTile`s (muted variant when the value is missing), About, and Amenities as a `<ul>` of ✓ tags.
62	- [ ] `src/App.jsx` -- the `/parks/:id` route renders `ParkDetails` for a found park (keep `onOpen` and `NotFound`); delete `ParkDetailsPlaceholder`.
63	- [ ] `src/data/format.test.js`, `src/App.test.jsx` -- unit-test the new helpers; test every matrix row (synthetic rows by editing the cloned sample in a custom fetch mock), section order, and focus on the heading after opening from the list.
64	
65	**Acceptance Criteria:**
66	- Given the list, when the user opens a park by keyboard, then the details `<h1>` has focus and is the only `<h1>` in the view.
67	- Given details, when the user activates "All parks", then the list renders and focus is on that park's card.
68	- Given a park with images, then one `<img>` shows with alt naming the park and a "Photo 1 of N" label.
69	- Given any of the 12 sample parks, when details render, then nothing crashes and no value reads "null", "undefined" or "NaN".

77	## Design Notes
78	
79	Focus-to-heading uses a ref plus `useEffect` keyed on `park.id`, so moving from one park to another also refocuses. The heading needs `tabIndex={-1}` to be focusable from code, and it gets no `focus-ring`, since a programmatic focus target is not a Tab stop. `PhotoArea` keeps a `failed` state and is keyed by park id, so a failure on one park does not hide another park's photo.
80	
81	Missing-value tile golden example:
82	
83	```jsx
84	<div className={missing ? 'border-dashed border-missing-line bg-missing-bg' : 'border-line bg-surface'}>
85	  <dt className="text-sm text-muted">Rating</dt>
86	  <dd className={missing ? 'italic text-muted' : 'font-semibold'}>{value ?? fallback}</dd>
87	</div>
88	```

97	- `npm run dev`. Open Cedar Hill: dashed photo box and a muted "Not yet rated" tile. Open Prospect Park: the photo shows "Photo 1 of 2". Tab to "All parks", press Enter, and focus lands on the card.
