# Epic 1 Context: Find a Park

<!-- Compiled from planning artifacts. Edit freely. Regenerate with compile-epic-context if planning docs change. -->

## Goal

Deliver a small, keyless web app (a 2-hour take-home) that lets a resident find a local park in the NYC-area sample set on a map or in a list and open its details, on phone or desktop, by mouse, touch, or keyboard, with missing data handled honestly. The interviewer must be able to run it with one install and one start command and judge it from the README. A modest app that works and is well explained beats an ambitious one with broken core features; stop at 2 hours and document what is unfinished.

## Stories

- Story 1.1: Scaffold app and park data layer
- Story 1.2: Park list with keyboard selection
- Story 1.3: Park details with missing-data handling
- Story 1.4: Map of parks synced with selection
- Story 1.5: Responsive and accessibility polish with design applied
- Story 1.6: Tests and submission README

## Requirements & Constraints

- **Cross-cutting (every story):** no geolocation ever requested; no credentials or API keys; never invent missing data; `parks.sample.json` is never edited (copied into the app byte-for-byte); keyboard focus always visible.
- The list plus details view must be a complete alternative to the map. All 12 parks appear in the list, including ones with missing fields; parks without valid lat/lng get no marker but stay in the list and details.
- Every one of the 12 parks renders; null/missing/empty fields are omitted or labelled, never crash the UI. Failed images degrade without a broken-image icon.
- A data-load failure shows an error message with retry, never a blank screen.
- At 375px and desktop widths, list → details → back works with no horizontal scroll.
- Unredacted AI transcripts (Claude Code, exported by the developer before each fresh story context, and Claude Design) and design files are part of the submission; the README must say where they live.
- Non-goals: backend, accounts, deployment, data editing. Search, filters, current location only if core is done with time to spare.
- Where the design and the spec disagree, the spec wins.

## Technical Decisions

- **Stack:** React + Vite, Tailwind CSS (mobile-first), `react-leaflet` + Leaflet with OpenStreetMap standard tiles (keyless, attribution visible), Vitest + React Testing Library. No component library.
- **Data loading (readiness decision):** the spec says static import, but loading/error/retry states require a mechanism that can actually fail. Use one that can (e.g. `fetch` from `public/` or a dynamic `import()`) and document the choice in the README.
- **Routing (readiness decision):** React Router. `/` is the list, `/parks/:id` is details (supersedes the design's `?park=` option). Unknown id shows "park not found" with a way back. Browser Back must work.
- **Data shape:** array of park objects with `id`, `name` (treat as required); `description`, `location.{lat,lng,address}`, `amenities` (kebab-case tokens), `hours` (free text, shown as written), `images` (URL array), `acreage`, `rating` (0–5) may each be missing, null, or empty. Read nested fields null-safely (`park.location?.address`).
- **Normalization:** one null-safe module applies safe defaults with no fabricated values, sorts parks A–Z and numbers them 1–N; that number is shared by list cards and map markers. Centralize fallback rules in a small helper (e.g. `display(value, fallback)`).
- **Image URL transformation:** pixel art moves from `design/park-images/` to `src/assets/park-images/`. At load time each image URL maps by filename stem to the bundled asset (`.../prospect-1.jpg` → `park-images/prospect-1.png`); unmatched URLs pass through unchanged so `onError` fallback still applies. Do not mutate source data. Document in README.
- **Amenity labels:** replace `-` with a space and capitalize the first letter (`dog-run` → "Dog run"); unknown tokens are humanized, never dropped.
- **Map:** initial view fits bounds of parks with coordinates. Leaflet default marker icons need explicit asset imports under Vite (or use `L.divIcon` with a `<button>` inside). Markers are focusable, named "N. Park name"; the selected marker is amber and larger.
- **Tailwind tokens:** paper `#F6F4EE`, surface `#FFFFFF`, line `#DAD6CB`, ink `#1B2620`, muted `#4F5B54`, forest `#1E5B3F` / dark `#123826`, tag bg `#E9EFE7` / fg `#23472F`, amber `#B45309`, missing bg `#F0EDE5` / line `#C9C4B6`. Font: Public Sans (Google Fonts, 400–800). Focus ring: `focus-visible:outline focus-visible:outline-[3px] focus-visible:outline-amber focus-visible:outline-offset-2`.
- **Tests must cover** every known data gap (see UX section), a failing image, an unmatched image URL, the URL transformation without mutation, and the keyboard list → details → back flow.

## UX & Interaction Patterns

- **Landmarks:** `<header>`, `<main>`, `<aside aria-label="Parks">`, map region labelled "Map of parks". One `<h1>` per view; no clickable `<div>`s; icon-only buttons have `aria-label`s; touch targets ≥ 44×44px; text contrast ≥ 4.5:1.
- **Desktop (≥ 768px):** green header (app name "Find a Park", subtitle, park count); ~400px independently scrolling sidebar; map fills the rest. Selecting a park switches the sidebar to details, starting with an "All parks" back control.
- **Phone (< 768px):** single column with a List / Map toggle of two `<button>`s using `aria-pressed`, List default. Tapping a marker shows a preview card ("Park N of 12", name, address, hours, "View park details", close button "Close park preview"). Details take the full screen with an "All parks" back link.
- **Focus management:** opening details moves focus to the details heading; returning to the list moves focus to the originating card.
- **Park card:** single `<button>`/link per card (one Tab stop) in a `<ul>`; number badge, name (17px bold), address, first three amenities as tags plus "+N more".
- **Details order:** back control, photo area ("Photo 1 of N", alt text naming the park), name as `<h1>` (26–28px, 800), address, "Open in OpenStreetMap" link from lat/lng, `<dl>` fact tiles (Hours, Size in acres, Rating "4.7 / 5"), About, all amenities as checked tags.
- **Missing-data copy:** address → "Address not listed" (muted); description null → "No description has been provided for this park."; rating null → "Not yet rated" (never 0/empty stars); empty or failed images → dashed box "No photos for this park yet"; hours/acreage → "Not listed"; empty amenities → hide section or "No amenities listed"; no lat/lng → no marker, no OSM link. Missing fact tiles use the muted style (missing bg, dashed border, italic), as on the phone screen; the desktop mock's normal-style "Not yet rated" tile is a mistake.
- **Sample gaps:** Old Mill Botanical Garden (null description), Cedar Hill Nature Preserve (empty images, null rating; coordinates look like a placeholder, note in Known issues), East Ridge Trailhead (empty images), Highland Dog Park (missing address).
- **Loading/error states:** "Loading parks…" and "Couldn't load parks. Try again." with a retry button, using the card style.
- Visual reference: `design/screens/` (1280×800 and 390×844). `design/source/*.dc.html` is reference only, not app code.

## Cross-Story Dependencies

- 1.1 provides the data layer, routing, layout shell, tokens, and bundled images that all later stories build on.
- 1.2 needs the 1.3 back control for its focus-return-to-card criterion. **Readiness decision:** in 1.2, verify focus return using browser Back; 1.3 confirms it via the "All parks" control.
- 1.4 relies on the shared A–Z numbering from 1.1 and the selected-park route to highlight markers and open details.
- 1.5 is the full responsive/accessibility pass over 1.2–1.4 (which ship baseline accessibility).
- 1.6 tests cover behaviour from 1.1–1.3; the README records decisions from all stories, including the data-load mechanism and React Router choice.
import { useCallback, useEffect, useState } from 'react'
import { Link, Route, Routes, useParams } from 'react-router'
import ParkList from './components/ParkList.jsx'
import { useParks } from './data/useParks.js'

const cardClass = 'rounded-[10px] border border-line bg-surface p-4'
const linkClass = 'focus-ring rounded text-forest underline'

function NotFound() {
  return (
    <div>
      <h1 className="text-lg font-bold">Park not found</h1>
      <p className="mt-2">
        <Link to="/" className={linkClass}>
          Back to all parks
        </Link>
      </p>
    </div>
  )
}

function ParkDetailsPlaceholder({ parks, onOpen }) {
  const { id } = useParams()
  const park = parks.find((p) => p.id === id)

  // Record the opened park so the list can return focus to its card.
  useEffect(() => {
    if (park) onOpen(park.id)
  }, [park, onOpen])

  if (!park) return <NotFound />

  return <h1 className="text-[28px] font-extrabold">{park.name}</h1>
}

function ParksPanel({ status, parks, retry, lastParkId, setLastParkId }) {
  const clearLastParkId = useCallback(() => setLastParkId(null), [setLastParkId])

  if (status === 'loading') {
    return (
      <p role="status" className={cardClass}>
        Loading parks…
      </p>
    )
  }

  if (status === 'error') {
    return (
      <div role="alert" className={cardClass}>
        <p>Couldn't load parks. Try again.</p>
        <button
          type="button"
          onClick={retry}
          className="focus-ring mt-3 min-h-11 rounded-lg bg-forest px-4 font-semibold text-white hover:bg-forest-dark"
        >
          Retry
        </button>
      </div>
    )
  }

  return (
    <Routes>
      <Route
        path="/"
        element={
          <ParkList parks={parks} focusParkId={lastParkId} onFocusUsed={clearLastParkId} />
        }
      />
      <Route
        path="/parks/:id"
        element={<ParkDetailsPlaceholder parks={parks} onOpen={setLastParkId} />}
      />
      <Route path="*" element={<NotFound />} />
    </Routes>
  )
}

export default function App() {
  const { status, parks, retry } = useParks()
  // Id of the last-opened park. Lives in App state (not location.state) so
  // focus can return to its card after browser Back, which carries no state.
  const [lastParkId, setLastParkId] = useState(null)

  return (
    <div className="flex min-h-screen flex-col">
      <header className="bg-forest px-5 py-4 text-white">
        <p className="text-xl font-extrabold">Find a Park</p>
        <p className="text-sm">New York City area</p>
        {status === 'ready' && <p className="text-sm">{parks.length} parks</p>}
      </header>
      <main className="flex flex-1 flex-col">
        <aside aria-label="Parks" className="p-5">
          <ParksPanel
            status={status}
            parks={parks}
            retry={retry}
            lastParkId={lastParkId}
            setLastParkId={setLastParkId}
          />
        </aside>
        <section aria-label="Map of parks" className="flex-1 p-5">
          <p className="text-muted">Map coming soon.</p>
        </section>
      </main>
    </div>
  )
}
/** Human label for a kebab-case amenity token: `dog-run` → "Dog run". Unknown tokens are humanized, never dropped. */
export function amenityLabel(token) {
  const spaced = String(token ?? '').trim().replace(/-/g, ' ')
  return spaced.charAt(0).toUpperCase() + spaced.slice(1)
}
// Null-safe park data layer. Normalization never invents values and never
// mutates the parsed source: missing scalars become `null`, missing arrays `[]`.

const imageModules = import.meta.glob('../assets/park-images/*.png', {
  eager: true,
  query: '?url',
  import: 'default',
})

// filename stem (e.g. "prospect-1") -> bundled asset URL
const bundledImages = Object.fromEntries(
  Object.entries(imageModules).map(([path, url]) => [fileStem(path), url]),
)

function fileStem(pathOrUrl) {
  const withoutQuery = pathOrUrl.split(/[?#]/)[0]
  const file = withoutQuery.split('/').pop() ?? ''
  const dot = file.lastIndexOf('.')
  return dot > 0 ? file.slice(0, dot) : file
}

/** Map a source image URL to the bundled art with the same filename stem; unmatched URLs pass through unchanged. */
export function resolveImageUrl(url) {
  if (typeof url !== 'string') return url
  return bundledImages[fileStem(url)] ?? url
}

function text(value) {
  if (typeof value !== 'string') return null
  const trimmed = value.trim()
  return trimmed !== '' ? trimmed : null
}

function finiteNumber(value) {
  return typeof value === 'number' && Number.isFinite(value) ? value : null
}

function numberInRange(value, min, max) {
  const n = finiteNumber(value)
  return n !== null && n >= min && n <= max ? n : null
}

function stringList(value) {
  return Array.isArray(value) ? value.filter((item) => text(item) !== null) : []
}

export function normalizePark(raw) {
  const park = raw && typeof raw === 'object' ? raw : {}
  const location = park.location && typeof park.location === 'object' ? park.location : {}
  const lat = numberInRange(location.lat, -90, 90)
  const lng = numberInRange(location.lng, -180, 180)
  const acreage = finiteNumber(park.acreage)
  const rating = finiteNumber(park.rating)

  return {
    id: text(park.id),
    name: text(park.name),
    number: null,
    description: text(park.description),
    address: text(location.address),
    lat,
    lng,
    hasCoords: lat !== null && lng !== null,
    amenities: stringList(park.amenities),
    hours: text(park.hours),
    images: stringList(park.images).map(resolveImageUrl),
    acreage: acreage !== null && acreage >= 0 ? acreage : null,
    rating: rating !== null && rating >= 0 && rating <= 5 ? rating : null,
  }
}

function byName(a, b) {
  if (a.name === null) return b.name === null ? 0 : 1
  if (b.name === null) return -1
  return a.name.localeCompare(b.name, 'en', { sensitivity: 'base' })
}

/** Normalize, sort A–Z by name and number 1–N. The number is assigned once here and shared by every view. */
export function normalizeParks(rawParks) {
  if (!Array.isArray(rawParks)) return []
  return rawParks
    .filter((raw) => raw && typeof raw === 'object')
    .map(normalizePark)
    .sort(byName)
    .map((park, index) => ({ ...park, number: index + 1 }))
}

export async function loadParks() {
  const response = await fetch(`${import.meta.env.BASE_URL}data/parks.sample.json`)
  if (!response.ok) {
    throw new Error(`Failed to load parks: HTTP ${response.status}`)
  }
  const data = await response.json()
  if (!Array.isArray(data)) {
    throw new Error('Failed to load parks: expected an array')
  }
  return normalizeParks(data)
}
@import "tailwindcss";

/* Design tokens from design/README.md */
@theme {
  --color-paper: #F6F4EE;
  --color-surface: #FFFFFF;
  --color-line: #DAD6CB;
  --color-ink: #1B2620;
  --color-muted: #4F5B54;
  --color-forest: #1E5B3F;
  --color-forest-dark: #123826;
  --color-tag-bg: #E9EFE7;
  --color-tag-fg: #23472F;
  --color-amber: #B45309;
  --color-missing-bg: #F0EDE5;
  --color-missing-line: #C9C4B6;

  --font-sans: "Public Sans", "Segoe UI", system-ui, sans-serif;
}

/* Amber focus ring: apply `focus-ring` to every interactive element. */
@utility focus-ring {
  &:focus-visible {
    outline-style: solid;
    outline-width: 3px;
    outline-color: var(--color-amber);
    outline-offset: 2px;
  }
}

body {
  @apply bg-paper text-ink font-sans;
}

# Find a Park: design handoff

UI designs for the Find a Park take-home. They were drawn on a Claude design canvas before any code was written, and they are the visual spec for the React + Tailwind + Leaflet build. The full AI transcript of the design session is in the repo alongside this folder.

**These are reference designs, not code.** Build with React, Tailwind and react-leaflet (no component library), and use the screenshots plus the rules below. Where the brief and these designs disagree, the brief wins.

## Contents

| File | What it shows |
|---|---|
| `screens/01-desktop-list-map.png` | Desktop default: park list on the left, map on the right |
| `screens/02-desktop-park-selected.png` | Desktop with a park selected: details replace the list, the marker is highlighted |
| `screens/03-desktop-missing-data.png` | Desktop details for a park with missing data (Cedar Hill: no photos, no rating) |
| `screens/04-phone-list.png` | Phone, List tab |
| `screens/05-phone-map-selected.png` | Phone, Map tab, with a park selected (preview card) |
| `screens/06-phone-details.png` | Phone details, full data (Prospect Park) |
| `screens/07-phone-missing-data.png` | Phone details, missing data (Cedar Hill) |
| `source/*.dc.html` | Canvas source files, for reference only. They need the canvas runtime and are **not** meant to run in the app. |

Screenshots are at 2x: desktop at 1280×800, phone at 390×844. The map in every screenshot is a drawn stand-in. The real app uses Leaflet with OpenStreetMap tiles.

## Layout and behaviour

### Desktop (≥ 768px, Tailwind `md:`)

- A green header with the app name, a subtitle and the park count.
- Two columns:
  - **Left sidebar (~400px):** scrolls on its own.
  - **Map:** fills the rest of the screen.
- **Sidebar, list mode:** an "All parks" heading, a one-line hint, then one card per park.
- **Selecting a park** from either the list or the map:
  - The sidebar switches to details mode, starting with an "All parks" back button.
  - That park's marker turns amber and gets bigger.
  - The URL should change too (e.g. `?park=prospect-park` or `/parks/:id`), so details can be linked and the browser Back button works.
- **Going back to the list:** move focus to the card of the park you came from.
- **Opening details:** move focus to the details heading, so keyboard and screen-reader users land in the right place.

### Phone (< 768px)

- One column. A **List / Map** toggle sits under the header, made of two real `<button>`s with `aria-pressed`. List is the default.
- **Tapping a marker** opens a preview card at the bottom of the map. It shows the park's position in the list ("Park 8 of 12"), its name, address and hours, a **View park details** button and a close button.
- **Details** take the whole screen, with an "All parks" back link in the header.

### List ↔ map link

- Parks are sorted A–Z and numbered 1–12. The same number appears on the list card and on the map marker, so the two views always match.
- Every marker is focusable and named "8. Prospect Park" for screen readers. Use `L.divIcon` with a `<button>` inside it, or set `keyboard: true` and `alt`/`title` on the markers.
- The list is the main way to choose a park. The map is never the only way.

## Park card (list)

- Number badge, park name (17px bold), address, and the first three amenities as tags. If there are more, add a "+N more" tag.
- The whole card is a single `<button>` (or a link once there is routing), so it gets keyboard focus and one Tab stop.

## Details

The details view shows, from top to bottom:

1. Photo area (see the missing-data rules below)
2. Park name as the page heading (26–28px, 800 weight), then the address
3. An **Open in OpenStreetMap** link built from the park's lat/lng (`https://www.openstreetmap.org/?mlat={lat}&mlon={lng}#map=16/{lat}/{lng}`). This was added during design and is easy to drop if you don't want it.
4. Three fact tiles: **Hours**, **Size** (acres), **Rating** ("4.7 / 5")
5. **About** (the description)
6. **Amenities**: all of them, as tags with a check mark. Turn the raw values into labels by replacing `-` with a space and capitalising the first letter (`dog-run` → "Dog run").

## Missing data: never invent a value

The brief requires this, and the sample data has every one of these cases.

| Field | When missing / null / empty | Show |
|---|---|---|
| `location.address` | Highland Dog Park | "Address not listed" in the secondary text colour |
| `description` | Old Mill Botanical Garden (`null`) | "No description has been provided for this park." |
| `rating` | Cedar Hill (`null`) | "Not yet rated" (do not show 0 or blank stars) |
| `images` | Cedar Hill, East Ridge (`[]`) | A dashed box: image icon + "No photos for this park yet" |
| `images` failing to load | **Every** sample URL (`images.example.com` doesn't exist) | The same fallback, triggered by `onError` on `<img>`; never a broken-image icon |
| `hours` | not in the sample, but handle it | "Not listed" |
| `acreage` | not in the sample, but handle it | "Not listed" |
| `amenities` | not in the sample, but handle it | Hide the Amenities section, or show "No amenities listed" |
| `location.lat/lng` | not in the sample, but handle it | Keep the park in the list, leave it off the map, and hide the map link |

Fact tiles with a missing value use the muted look from screen 07: `#F0EDE5` background, dashed `#C9C4B6` border, italic text. *Remark: the desktop screenshot (03) shows "Not yet rated" with the normal tile style. That is a mistake in the mock, and the phone version is the intended look.*

Wrap every field in a small helper (e.g. `display(value, fallback)`) so the rules stay in one place. Never let a missing nested field crash the app (`park.location?.address`).

## Design tokens (Tailwind)

Add these under `theme.extend` (or as CSS variables with Tailwind v4 `@theme`):

```js
colors: {
  paper:   '#F6F4EE', // page background
  surface: '#FFFFFF', // cards, tiles
  line:    '#DAD6CB', // card borders
  ink:     '#1B2620', // main text
  muted:   '#4F5B54', // secondary text (≈7:1 on white)
  forest:  { DEFAULT: '#1E5B3F', dark: '#123826' }, // header, primary buttons, markers, links
  tag:     { bg: '#E9EFE7', fg: '#23472F' },         // amenity tags
  amber:   '#B45309', // selected marker and focus ring
  missing: { bg: '#F0EDE5', line: '#C9C4B6' },       // missing-value tiles, no-photo box
  map:     { land: '#E4E8DF', water: '#B9D3DC' },    // only for the drawn map; real tiles replace these
},
fontFamily: { sans: ['"Public Sans"', '"Segoe UI"', 'system-ui', 'sans-serif'] },
```

- **Font:** Public Sans from Google Fonts, weights 400/500/600/700/800.
- **Type sizes:**
  - Body 15–16px; card titles 17px bold
  - Details heading 26–28px (800 weight); section headings 17–18px
  - Small labels 13px; nothing smaller except the map attribution
- **Corner radius:** cards 10px, buttons 8–10px, tags fully rounded.
- **Spacing:** sidebar padding 20px (16px on phone), 10px between cards, 14–16px inside cards.

## Accessibility

These checks match what the brief evaluates:

- **Focus ring:** every interactive element gets a visible ring, `focus-visible:outline focus-visible:outline-[3px] focus-visible:outline-amber focus-visible:outline-offset-2`. Never remove outlines without putting this ring in their place.
- **Touch targets:** at least 44×44px, including the zoom buttons, the close button and the back button.
- **Real elements:** use `<button>`, `<a href>`, `<ul>/<li>` for the list, `<dl>/<dt>/<dd>` for the fact tiles, and one `<h1>` per view. No clickable `<div>`s.
- **Landmarks:** `<header>`, `<main>`, an `<aside aria-label="Parks">` for the sidebar, and a map section labelled "Map of parks".
- **Labels:** icon-only buttons need an `aria-label` ("Zoom in", "Close park preview").
- **Contrast:** text has at least 4.5:1 contrast. The colours above meet that, including white on forest.
- **Location:** never ask for the user's location; the app must work without it. Centre the map on a fixed view, or fit it to the parks' bounds.

## Remarks and open decisions

- **Municipality:** the parks span Brooklyn, Manhattan, Queens and the Bronx and are mostly fictional (Prospect Park is real). The designs call the area "New York City area". Say so in the project README.
- **Cedar Hill coordinates** (40.7128, -74.006) are exactly the standard "New York City" point, so they are probably a placeholder. The park is shown as given; mention it in Known issues.
- **Short addresses** such as "Ridge Rd" and "Shore Pkwy" are shown as given, without adding a city.
- **Not designed:**
  - Search and filters (optional in the brief)
  - Current-location support (optional)
  - A loading state and an error state for when `parks.json` fails to load. Both are worth adding: a short "Loading parks…" message and a "Couldn't load parks. Try again." message with a retry button, using the same card style.
- **Hours** are shown exactly as written in the data ("Dawn to dusk", "24 hours"). An "Open now" badge was left out on purpose, because parsing these strings reliably is out of scope.
- **Photos:** there is no gallery. The mock shows the first photo with a "Photo 1 of N" label. Showing a single image, or a simple row of thumbnails, is fine.
- **Map tiles:** OpenStreetMap's tile server needs no API key; keep its attribution visible. For real public use, switch to a tile provider that allows production traffic. That belongs in the README's next steps.

import { Fragment, useEffect, useRef } from 'react'
import { Link } from 'react-router'
import { amenityLabel } from '../data/format.js'

const MAX_TAGS = 3

function ParkCard({ park, linkRef }) {
  const shown = park.amenities.slice(0, MAX_TAGS)
  const extra = park.amenities.length - shown.length
  const tags = shown.map(amenityLabel)
  if (extra > 0) tags.push(`+${extra} more`)

  return (
    <li>
      <Link
        ref={linkRef}
        to={`/parks/${park.id}`}
        className="focus-ring flex min-h-11 items-start gap-3 rounded-[10px] border border-line bg-surface p-4 hover:border-forest"
      >
        <span
          aria-hidden="true"
          className="flex size-8 shrink-0 items-center justify-center rounded-full bg-forest text-sm font-bold text-white"
        >
          {park.number}
        </span>
        <span className="sr-only">{park.number}.</span>{' '}
        <span className="min-w-0 flex-1">
          <span className="block text-[17px] font-bold">{park.name}</span>{' '}
          {park.address ? (
            <span className="block text-muted">{park.address}</span>
          ) : (
            <span className="block text-muted">Address not listed</span>
          )}{' '}
          {tags.length > 0 && (
            <span data-testid="park-tags" className="mt-2 flex flex-wrap gap-2">
              {tags.map((label, index) => (
                <Fragment key={`${index}-${label}`}>
                  <span className="rounded-full bg-tag-bg px-2.5 py-0.5 text-sm text-tag-fg">
                    {label}
                  </span>{' '}
                </Fragment>
              ))}
            </span>
          )}
        </span>
        <svg
          aria-hidden="true"
          viewBox="0 0 24 24"
          className="size-5 shrink-0 self-center text-muted"
          fill="none"
          stroke="currentColor"
          strokeWidth="2"
        >
          <path d="m9 6 6 6-6 6" />
        </svg>
      </Link>
    </li>
  )
}

/**
 * The "All parks" list. Parks are rendered in loader order with loader numbers.
 * If `focusParkId` matches a card on mount, that card's link gets focus and
 * `onFocusUsed` is called so a later fresh visit does not steal focus.
 */
export default function ParkList({ parks, focusParkId, onFocusUsed }) {
  const linkRefs = useRef(new Map())

  useEffect(() => {
    if (!focusParkId) return
    linkRefs.current.get(focusParkId)?.focus()
    onFocusUsed?.()
    // Run once on mount: the id is read once, then cleared by the parent.
  }, [])

  return (
    <div>
      <h1 className="text-lg font-bold">All parks</h1>
      <p className="mt-1 text-muted">Choose a park to see hours, amenities and directions.</p>
      <ul className="mt-4 flex flex-col gap-3">
        {parks.map((park) => (
          <ParkCard
            key={park.id}
            park={park}
            linkRef={(node) => {
              if (node) linkRefs.current.set(park.id, node)
              else linkRefs.current.delete(park.id)
            }}
          />
        ))}
      </ul>
    </div>
  )
}
import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs'
import { resolve } from 'node:path'
import { render, screen, within } from '@testing-library/react'
import userEvent from '@testing-library/user-event'
import { MemoryRouter, useNavigate } from 'react-router'
import { afterEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from 'vitest'
import App from './App.jsx'

const sample = JSON.parse(
  readFileSync(resolve(process.cwd(), 'public/data/parks.sample.json'), 'utf8'),
)

const okResponse = () => ({ ok: true, json: async () => structuredClone(sample) })
const ERROR_TEXT = "Couldn't load parks. Try again."

function renderAt(path) {
  return render(
    <MemoryRouter initialEntries={[path]}>
      <App />
    </MemoryRouter>,
  )
}

afterEach(() => vi.unstubAllGlobals())

describe('App shell', () => {
  it('goes loading → error → retry → ready', async () => {
    let rejectFirst
    const fetchMock = vi
      .fn()
      .mockImplementationOnce(
        () =>
          new Promise((_resolve, reject) => {
            rejectFirst = reject
          }),
      )
      .mockResolvedValueOnce(okResponse())
    vi.stubGlobal('fetch', fetchMock)
    const user = userEvent.setup()

    renderAt('/')
    const aside = screen.getByRole('complementary', { name: 'Parks' })
    expect(within(aside).getByText('Loading parks…')).toBeInTheDocument()

    rejectFirst(new TypeError('network down'))
    expect(await within(aside).findByText(ERROR_TEXT)).toBeInTheDocument()

    await user.click(within(aside).getByRole('button', { name: 'Retry' }))
    expect(await within(aside).findByRole('heading', { name: 'All parks' })).toBeInTheDocument()
    expect(screen.getByText('12 parks')).toBeInTheDocument()
    expect(fetchMock).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(2)
  })

  it('shows the error state for a non-OK response', async () => {
    vi.stubGlobal('fetch', vi.fn().mockResolvedValue({ ok: false, status: 404 }))
    renderAt('/')
    expect(await screen.findByText(ERROR_TEXT)).toBeInTheDocument()
    expect(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Retry' })).toBeInTheDocument()
  })

  it('renders the landmarks', async () => {
    vi.stubGlobal('fetch', vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(okResponse()))
    renderAt('/')
    expect(screen.getByRole('banner')).toBeInTheDocument()
    expect(screen.getByRole('main')).toBeInTheDocument()
    expect(screen.getByRole('complementary', { name: 'Parks' })).toBeInTheDocument()
    expect(screen.getByRole('region', { name: 'Map of parks' })).toBeInTheDocument()
    expect(await screen.findByRole('heading', { level: 1, name: 'All parks' })).toBeInTheDocument()
  })

  it('shows the park name on /parks/:id', async () => {
    vi.stubGlobal('fetch', vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(okResponse()))
    renderAt('/parks/prospect-park')
    expect(
      await screen.findByRole('heading', { level: 1, name: 'Prospect Park' }),
    ).toBeInTheDocument()
  })

  it('shows "Park not found" with a link back for an unmatched path', async () => {
    vi.stubGlobal('fetch', vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(okResponse()))
    renderAt('/foo')
    expect(await screen.findByRole('heading', { name: 'Park not found' })).toBeInTheDocument()
    expect(screen.getByRole('link', { name: 'Back to all parks' })).toHaveAttribute('href', '/')
  })

  it('shows "Park not found" with a link back for an unknown id', async () => {
    vi.stubGlobal('fetch', vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(okResponse()))
    const user = userEvent.setup()
    renderAt('/parks/nope')
    expect(await screen.findByRole('heading', { name: 'Park not found' })).toBeInTheDocument()
    await user.click(screen.getByRole('link', { name: 'Back to all parks' }))
    expect(await screen.findByRole('heading', { name: 'All parks' })).toBeInTheDocument()
  })

  it('returns to / on history Back after navigating to a park', async () => {
    vi.stubGlobal('fetch', vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(okResponse()))
    const user = userEvent.setup()

    function TestNav() {
      const navigate = useNavigate()
      return (
        <>
          <button type="button" onClick={() => navigate('/parks/prospect-park')}>
            go
          </button>
          <button type="button" onClick={() => navigate(-1)}>
            back
          </button>
        </>
      )
    }

    render(
      <MemoryRouter initialEntries={['/']}>
        <TestNav />
        <App />
      </MemoryRouter>,
    )
    expect(await screen.findByRole('heading', { name: 'All parks' })).toBeInTheDocument()
    await user.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'go' }))
    expect(await screen.findByRole('heading', { name: 'Prospect Park' })).toBeInTheDocument()
    await user.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'back' }))
    expect(await screen.findByRole('heading', { name: 'All parks' })).toBeInTheDocument()
  })
})

const SORTED_NAMES = [
  'Cedar Hill Nature Preserve',
  'Central Plaza Green',
  'East Ridge Trailhead',
  'Highland Dog Park',
  'Hillcrest Skate Park',
  'Lakeshore Point',
  'Old Mill Botanical Garden',
  'Prospect Park',
  'Riverside Commons',
  'Sunset Playground',
  'Veterans Memorial Field',
  'Willow Creek Wetlands',
]

async function findCards() {
  const list = await screen.findByRole('list')
  return within(list).getAllByRole('listitem')
}

function cardFor(name) {
  return screen
    .getAllByRole('listitem')
    .find((li) => within(li).queryByText(name, { exact: true }) !== null)
}

describe('Park list', () => {
  it('renders all 12 parks in A–Z order inside one <ul> under the h1 and hint', async () => {
    vi.stubGlobal('fetch', vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(okResponse()))
    renderAt('/')
    const cards = await findCards()
    expect(screen.getAllByRole('list')).toHaveLength(1)
    expect(cards).toHaveLength(12)

    const heading = screen.getByRole('heading', { level: 1, name: 'All parks' })
    const hint = screen.getByText('Choose a park to see hours, amenities and directions.')
    const list = screen.getByRole('list')
    expect(list.tagName).toBe('UL')
    expect(heading.compareDocumentPosition(hint) & Node.DOCUMENT_POSITION_FOLLOWING).toBeTruthy()
    expect(hint.compareDocumentPosition(list) & Node.DOCUMENT_POSITION_FOLLOWING).toBeTruthy()

    cards.forEach((card, i) => {
      const links = within(card).getAllByRole('link')
      expect(links).toHaveLength(1)
      expect(links[0]).toHaveAccessibleName(
        expect.stringMatching(new RegExp(`^${i + 1}\\. ${SORTED_NAMES[i]} `)),
      )
      expect(within(card).getByText(String(i + 1))).toBeInTheDocument()
      expect(within(card).getByText(SORTED_NAMES[i])).toBeInTheDocument()
      expect(within(card).queryByRole('button')).toBeNull()
    })
  })

  it('links each card to /parks/:id with the focus-ring class and a 44px target', async () => {
    vi.stubGlobal('fetch', vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(okResponse()))
    renderAt('/')
    await findCards()
    const link = within(cardFor('Prospect Park')).getByRole('link')
    expect(link).toHaveAttribute('href', '/parks/prospect-park')
    expect(link).toHaveClass('focus-ring')
    expect(link).toHaveClass('min-h-11')
  })

  it('shows "Address not listed" in muted text for a missing address', async () => {
    vi.stubGlobal('fetch', vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(okResponse()))
    renderAt('/')
    await findCards()
    const fallback = within(cardFor('Highland Dog Park')).getByText('Address not listed')
    expect(fallback).toHaveClass('text-muted')
    expect(within(cardFor('Prospect Park')).getByText('Brooklyn, NY 11225')).toBeInTheDocument()
    expect(within(cardFor('Prospect Park')).queryByText('Address not listed')).toBeNull()
  })

  it('shows the first 3 amenities plus "+N more" when there are more than 3', async () => {
    vi.stubGlobal('fetch', vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(okResponse()))
    renderAt('/')
    await findCards()
    const card = cardFor('Prospect Park')
    expect(within(card).getByText('Playground')).toBeInTheDocument()
    expect(within(card).getByText('Dog run')).toBeInTheDocument()
    expect(within(card).getByText('Trails')).toBeInTheDocument()
    expect(within(card).queryByText('Restrooms')).toBeNull()
    expect(within(card).getByText('+4 more')).toBeInTheDocument()
  })

  it('shows all amenities and no "+N more" when there are 3 or fewer', async () => {
    vi.stubGlobal('fetch', vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(okResponse()))
    renderAt('/')
    await findCards()
    const card = cardFor('East Ridge Trailhead')
    expect(within(card).getByText('Trails')).toBeInTheDocument()
    expect(within(card).getByText('Parking')).toBeInTheDocument()
    expect(within(card).getByText('Wildlife viewing')).toBeInTheDocument()
    expect(within(card).queryByText(/more$/)).toBeNull()
  })

  it('humanizes amenity labels', async () => {
    vi.stubGlobal('fetch', vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(okResponse()))
    renderAt('/')
    await findCards()
    expect(within(cardFor('Highland Dog Park')).getByText('Dog run')).toBeInTheDocument()
    expect(within(cardFor('Central Plaza Green')).getByText('Wifi')).toBeInTheDocument()
    expect(within(cardFor('Central Plaza Green')).getByText('Event lawn')).toBeInTheDocument()
  })

  it('renders no tag row for a park with no amenities', async () => {
    const data = structuredClone(sample)
    data.find((p) => p.id === 'east-ridge-trailhead').amenities = []
    vi.stubGlobal('fetch', vi.fn().mockResolvedValue({ ok: true, json: async () => data }))
    renderAt('/')
    await findCards()
    const card = cardFor('East Ridge Trailhead')
    expect(within(card).queryByText('Trails')).toBeNull()
    expect(within(card).queryByTestId('park-tags')).toBeNull()
  })

  it('does not auto-focus any card on a first visit', async () => {
    vi.stubGlobal('fetch', vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(okResponse()))
    renderAt('/')
    await findCards()
    expect(document.activeElement).toBe(document.body)
  })

  it('gives each card exactly one Tab stop', async () => {
    vi.stubGlobal('fetch', vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(okResponse()))
    const user = userEvent.setup()
    renderAt('/')
    const cards = await findCards()
    for (const card of cards) {
      await user.tab()
      expect(document.activeElement).toBe(within(card).getByRole('link'))
    }
  })

  it('opens details when Enter is pressed on a focused card', async () => {
    vi.stubGlobal('fetch', vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(okResponse()))
    const user = userEvent.setup()
    renderAt('/')
    await findCards()
    await user.tab()
    expect(document.activeElement).toHaveAttribute('href', '/parks/cedar-hill-nature-preserve')
    await user.keyboard('{Enter}')
    expect(
      await screen.findByRole('heading', { level: 1, name: 'Cedar Hill Nature Preserve' }),
    ).toBeInTheDocument()
    expect(screen.queryByRole('list')).toBeNull()
  })

    vi.stubGlobal('fetch', vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(okResponse()))
    const user = userEvent.setup()
    renderAt('/')
    await findCards()

    expect(
      await screen.findByRole('heading', { level: 1, name: 'Prospect Park' }),
    ).toBeInTheDocument()
  })

  it('returns focus to the originating card on history Back, and only once', async () => {
    vi.stubGlobal('fetch', vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(okResponse()))
    const user = userEvent.setup()

    function TestNav() {
      const navigate = useNavigate()
      return (
        <>
          <button type="button" onClick={() => navigate(-1)}>
            back
          </button>
          <button type="button" onClick={() => navigate('/parks/nope')}>
            elsewhere
          </button>
          <button type="button" onClick={() => navigate('/')}>
            home
          </button>
        </>
      )
    }

    render(
      <MemoryRouter initialEntries={['/']}>
        <TestNav />
        <App />
      </MemoryRouter>,
    )
    await findCards()
    await user.click(within(cardFor('Highland Dog Park')).getByRole('link'))
    expect(await screen.findByRole('heading', { name: 'Highland Dog Park' })).toBeInTheDocument()

    await user.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'back' }))
    await findCards()
    expect(document.activeElement).toBe(within(cardFor('Highland Dog Park')).getByRole('link'))

    // The id was consumed: a later fresh visit to / does not steal focus.
    await user.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'elsewhere' }))
    expect(await screen.findByRole('heading', { name: 'Park not found' })).toBeInTheDocument()
    await user.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'home' }))
    await findCards()
    expect(screen.getAllByRole('listitem').map((li) => within(li).getByRole('link'))).not.toContain(
      document.activeElement,
    )
  })
})
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest'
import { amenityLabel } from './format.js'

describe('amenityLabel', () => {
  it('replaces dashes with spaces and capitalizes the first letter', () => {
    expect(amenityLabel('dog-run')).toBe('Dog run')
    expect(amenityLabel('wildlife-viewing')).toBe('Wildlife viewing')
  })

  it('capitalizes single-word tokens', () => {
    expect(amenityLabel('wifi')).toBe('Wifi')
  })

  it('humanizes unknown tokens instead of dropping them', () => {
    expect(amenityLabel('zip-line-tower')).toBe('Zip line tower')
  })

  it('keeps the rest of the token as written', () => {
    expect(amenityLabel('BBQ-pits')).toBe('BBQ pits')
  })

  it('trims surrounding whitespace before transforming', () => {
    expect(amenityLabel(' dog-run ')).toBe('Dog run')
  })
})

'acre' : 'acres'}`\n}\n\n/** OpenStreetMap link centred on a point. */\nexport function osmUrl(lat, lng) {\n  return `https://www.openstreetmap.org/?mlat=${lat}&mlon=${lng}#map=16/${lat}/${lng}`\n}\n\n/**\n * The one fallback rule: a value that is null, undefined or blank text is\n * missing, and shows the labelled fallback instead. Never invents a value.\n */\nexport function display(value, fallback) {\n  const missing =\n    value === null ||\n    value === undefined ||\n    (typeof value === 'string' && value.trim() === '') ||\n    (typeof value === 'number' && !Number.isFinite(value))\n  return missing ? { text: fallback, missing: true } : { text: String(value), missing: false }\n}\nEOF"

+function tile(label) {

+import { useEffect, useRef, useState } from 'react'
+import { Link } from 'react-router'
+import { amenityLabel, display, formatAcres, formatRating, osmUrl } from '../data/format.js'
+
+// Fallback copy, word for word from design/README.md.
+const NO_ADDRESS = 'Address not listed'
+const NO_DESCRIPTION = 'No description has been provided for this park.'
+const NO_RATING = 'Not yet rated'
+const NO_PHOTOS = 'No photos for this park yet'
+const NOT_LISTED = 'Not listed'
+const NO_AMENITIES = 'No amenities listed'
+
+function NoPhoto() {
+  return (
+    <div
+      data-testid="no-photo"
+      className="flex aspect-[16/9] flex-col items-center justify-center gap-2 rounded-[10px] border-2 border-dashed border-missing-line bg-missing-bg p-4 text-center text-muted"
+    >
+      <svg
+        aria-hidden="true"
+        viewBox="0 0 24 24"
+        className="size-7"
+        fill="none"
+        stroke="currentColor"
+        strokeWidth="1.75"
+        strokeLinecap="round"
+        strokeLinejoin="round"
+      >
+        <rect x="3" y="4" width="18" height="16" rx="2" />
+        <path d="m3 16 5-5 4 4 3-3 6 6" />
+        <circle cx="15.5" cy="8.5" r="1.5" />
+      </svg>
+      <p>{NO_PHOTOS}</p>
+    </div>
+  )
+}
+
+/** First photo only, with a "Photo 1 of N" label. A load failure swaps in the no-photo box. */
+function PhotoArea({ park }) {
+  const [failed, setFailed] = useState(false)
+  const src = park.images[0]
+
+  if (!src || failed) return <NoPhoto />
+
+  return (
+    <figure className="relative overflow-hidden rounded-[10px] bg-missing-bg">
+      <img
+        src={src}
+        alt={`Photo of ${park.name}`}
+        onError={() => setFailed(true)}
+        className="block aspect-[16/9] w-full object-cover"
+      />
+      <figcaption className="absolute right-2 bottom-2 rounded-full bg-ink/85 px-2.5 py-0.5 text-[13px] font-semibold text-white">
+        Photo 1 of {park.images.length}
+      </figcaption>
+    </figure>
+  )
+}
+
+function FactTile({ label, value }) {
+  const { missing, text } = value
+  return (
+    <div
+      className={`min-w-0 break-words rounded-[10px] border p-3 ${
+        missing ? 'border-dashed border-missing-line bg-missing-bg' : 'border-line bg-surface'
+      }`}
+    >
+      <dt className="text-sm text-muted">{label}</dt>
+      <dd className={missing ? 'italic text-muted' : 'font-semibold'}>{text}</dd>
+    </div>
+  )
+}
+
+/** Full details for one normalized park. Every missing value shows its labelled fallback. */
+export default function ParkDetails({ park }) {
+  const headingRef = useRef(null)
+
+  // Opening details (or moving to another park) puts focus on the heading.
+  useEffect(() => {
+    headingRef.current?.focus()
+  }, [park.id])
+
+  const address = display(park.address, NO_ADDRESS)
+  const about = display(park.description, NO_DESCRIPTION)
+
+  return (
+    <article>
+      <Link
+        to="/"
+        className="focus-ring inline-flex min-h-11 min-w-11 items-center gap-1 rounded-lg border border-line bg-surface px-3 font-semibold text-forest hover:border-forest"
+      >
+        <svg
+          aria-hidden="true"
+          viewBox="0 0 24 24"
+          className="size-5"
+          fill="none"
+          stroke="currentColor"
+          strokeWidth="2"
+        >
+          <path d="m15 6-6 6 6 6" />
+        </svg>
+        All parks
+      </Link>
+
+      <div className="mt-4">
+        <PhotoArea key={park.id} park={park} />
+      </div>
+
+      <h1
+        ref={headingRef}
+        tabIndex={-1}
+        className="mt-4 text-[28px] leading-tight font-extrabold"
+      >
+        {park.name}
+      </h1>
+      <p className="mt-1 text-muted">{address.text}</p>
+
+      {park.hasCoords && (
+        <p className="mt-2">
+          <a
+            href={osmUrl(park.lat, park.lng)}
+            target="_blank"
+            rel="noopener noreferrer"
+            className="focus-ring inline-flex min-h-11 items-center rounded font-semibold text-forest underline"
+          >
+            Open in OpenStreetMap
+          </a>
+        </p>
+      )}
+
+      <dl className="mt-3 grid grid-cols-3 gap-2">
+        <FactTile label="Hours" value={display(park.hours, NOT_LISTED)} />
+        <FactTile label="Size" value={display(formatAcres(park.acreage), NOT_LISTED)} />
+        <FactTile label="Rating" value={display(formatRating(park.rating), NO_RATING)} />
+      </dl>
+
+      <section aria-labelledby="park-about" className="mt-5">
+        <h2 id="park-about" className="text-lg font-bold">
+          About
+        </h2>
+        <p className={`mt-1 ${about.missing ? 'italic text-muted' : ''}`}>{about.text}</p>
+      </section>
+
+      <section aria-labelledby="park-amenities" className="mt-5">
+        <h2 id="park-amenities" className="text-lg font-bold">
+          Amenities
+        </h2>
+        {park.amenities.length > 0 ? (
+          <ul aria-label="Amenities" className="mt-2 flex flex-wrap gap-2">
+            {park.amenities.map((token, index) => (
+              <li
+                key={`${index}-${token}`}
+                className="inline-flex items-center gap-1.5 rounded-full bg-tag-bg px-3 py-1 text-tag-fg"
+              >
+                <svg
+                  aria-hidden="true"
+                  viewBox="0 0 24 24"
+                  className="size-4"
+                  fill="none"
+                  stroke="currentColor"
+                  strokeWidth="2.5"
+                >
+                  <path d="m5 12 5 5 9-10" />
+                </svg>
+                {amenityLabel(token)}
+              </li>
+            ))}
+          </ul>
+        ) : (
+          <p className="mt-1 italic text-muted">{NO_AMENITIES}</p>
+        )}
+      </section>
+    </article>
+  )
+}
diff --git a/src/data/format.js b/src/data/format.js
index b16586b..5bb8421 100644
--- a/src/data/format.js
+++ b/src/data/format.js
@@ -3,3 +3,33 @@ export function amenityLabel(token) {
   const spaced = String(token ?? '').trim().replace(/-/g, ' ')
   return spaced.charAt(0).toUpperCase() + spaced.slice(1)
 }
+
+/** Rating in 0–5 as "4.7 / 5" (one decimal); `null` when there is no valid rating. */
+export function formatRating(n) {
+  if (typeof n !== 'number' || !Number.isFinite(n)) return null
+  return `${n.toFixed(1)} / 5`
+}
+
+/** Acreage as "526 acres" or "1 acre"; `null` when there is no valid acreage. */
+export function formatAcres(n) {
+  if (typeof n !== 'number' || !Number.isFinite(n)) return null
+  return `${n} ${n === 1 ? 'acre' : 'acres'}`
+}
+
+/** OpenStreetMap link centred on a point. */
+export function osmUrl(lat, lng) {
+  return `https://www.openstreetmap.org/?mlat=${lat}&mlon=${lng}#map=16/${lat}/${lng}`
+}
+
+/**
+ * The one fallback rule: a value that is null, undefined or blank text is
+ * missing, and shows the labelled fallback instead. Never invents a value.
+ */
+export function display(value, fallback) {
+  const missing =
+    value === null ||
+    value === undefined ||
+    (typeof value === 'string' && value.trim() === '') ||
+    (typeof value === 'number' && !Number.isFinite(value))
+  return missing ? { text: fallback, missing: true } : { text: String(value), missing: false }
+}

+describe('formatRating', () => {
+  it('shows one decimal out of 5', () => {
+    expect(formatRating(4.7)).toBe('4.7 / 5')
+    expect(formatRating(4)).toBe('4.0 / 5')
+    expect(formatRating(0)).toBe('0.0 / 5')
+  })
+
+  it('returns null for a missing or invalid rating', () => {
+    expect(formatRating(null)).toBeNull()
+    expect(formatRating(undefined)).toBeNull()
+    expect(formatRating(NaN)).toBeNull()
+    expect(formatRating('4.5')).toBeNull()
+  })
+})
+
+describe('formatAcres', () => {
+  it('pluralizes acres', () => {
+    expect(formatAcres(526)).toBe('526 acres')
+    expect(formatAcres(1)).toBe('1 acre')
+    expect(formatAcres(0.5)).toBe('0.5 acres')
+  })
+
+  it('returns null for a missing or invalid acreage', () => {
+    expect(formatAcres(null)).toBeNull()
+    expect(formatAcres(undefined)).toBeNull()
+    expect(formatAcres(Infinity)).toBeNull()
+  })
+})

+    )
+  })

+describe('display', () => {
+  it('passes a present value through as text', () => {
+    expect(display('Dawn to dusk', 'Not listed')).toEqual({ text: 'Dawn to dusk', missing: false })
+    expect(display(0, 'Not listed')).toEqual({ text: '0', missing: false })
+  })
+
+  it('uses the fallback for null, undefined, blank text and NaN', () => {
+    for (const value of [null, undefined, '', '   ', NaN]) {
+      expect(display(value, 'Not listed')).toEqual({ text: 'Not listed', missing: true })
+    }
+  })

69	
70	   For each finding:
71	   - A gap finding from the verification-gap layer arrives pre-verified — that layer's evidence rules made it read the tests and run the searches it cites, and triage trusts the claim as filed. Skip verification, render the verdict from the filed evidence, and weigh its filed disposition when routing. Its `Other findings` are verified like everything else.
72	   - **Verify the finding's claim.** At the cited file and line, does the bad outcome the reviewer describes actually occur? Read beyond the changed lines — follow callers, guards upstream, etc — until you can answer yes or no. A different finding about nearby code does not settle this one. Judge whether the problem is real, not whether the proposed fix is plausible. Code that loudly fails on a situation you never showed the program can reach is correct behavior, not a defect.
73	   - **Render exactly one verdict** from what verification established — the verdict is the whole triage decision; there is no separate keep-or-dismiss.
74	     - `high` (intolerable), `medium` (tolerable), `low` (cosmetic or negligible) — the bad outcome is real. Assign severity by how much it hurts end users or developers. For developer-only problems (inconsistent design, eroded invariants, duplicated sources of truth), name where it will cause trouble — which caller will diverge, which rule will break. A vague "this is messy" with no named harm is not a severity grade; use `false` or `maybe-false` instead. When the harm is real but you cannot tell how bad, pick the higher grade.
75	     - `false` — you checked, and the bad outcome does not happen at the cited location. Write what disproves this specific claim. A true fact about nearby code that does not disprove the claim does not count.
76	     - `maybe-false` — you could not tell whether the bad outcome happens. Write what you would need to check to find out. Use this only when the diff and surrounding code leave the question open; when they are enough to decide, pick `high`, `medium`, `low`, or `false`.
77	
78	   - Every finding gets one row in the `## Review Triage Log` section of `{spec_file}` — verdict plus its evidence in a sentence or two; never drop, merge, or silently skip one.
79	
80	   Reject `false` findings on their refutation.
81	
82	   Reject `low` findings when it is unlikely that users or developers would meet the defect in everyday use (judged plainly — no proof needed) and the fix is more than a direct correction or deletion — adding guards, branches, parameters, or other complexity.
83	
84	   Out of scope: reject or defer a finding as out of scope only when the intent itself excludes it — not because the spec's scope section, the plan, or the shape of the diff says so. 1	# Edge Case Hunter Review
2	
3	**Goal:** You are a pure path tracer. Never comment on whether code is good or bad; only list missing handling.
4	When a diff is provided, scan only the diff hunks and list boundaries that are directly reachable from the changed lines and lack an explicit guard in the diff.
5	When no diff is provided (full file or function), treat the entire provided content as the scope.
6	Ignore the rest of the codebase unless the provided content explicitly references external functions.
7	A brief secondary deletion check runs as Step 4 when the diff removes code.
8	A claims check runs as Step 5.
9	
10	**Inputs:**
11	- **content** — Content to review, or a path to read it from: diff, full file, or function
12	- **also_consider** (optional) — Areas to keep in mind during review alongside normal edge-case analysis
13	- **claims_file** — Path to the spec this change was built from. Do NOT read it before Step 5: the path tracing in Steps 2–3 must finish before the claims are seen.
14	
15	**MANDATORY: Execute steps in the Execution section IN EXACT ORDER. DO NOT skip steps or change the sequence. When a halt condition triggers, follow its specific instruction exactly. Each action within a step is a REQUIRED action to complete that step.**
16	
17	**Your method is exhaustive path enumeration — mechanically walk every branch, not hunt by intuition. Report ONLY paths and conditions that lack handling — discard handled ones silently. Do NOT editorialize or add filler. Do not assign severity labels, rankings, or priority levels.**
18	
19	
20	## EXECUTION
21	
22	### Step 1: Receive Content
23	
24	- Take the content to review from the parent message that launched you — inline, or by reading the file it points to (never from this instruction file)
25	- If no content is supplied, or it is empty, unreadable, or cannot be decoded as text, return `[{"location":"N/A","trigger_condition":"Input empty or undecodable","guard_snippet":"Provide valid content to review","potential_consequence":"Review skipped — no analysis performed"}]` and stop
26	- Identify content type (diff, full file, or function) to determine scope rules
27	
28	### Step 2: Exhaustive Path Analysis
29	
30	**Walk every branching path and boundary condition within scope — report only unhandled ones.**
31	
32	- If `also_consider` input was provided, incorporate those areas into the analysis
33	- Walk all branching paths: control flow (conditionals, loops, error handlers, early returns) and domain boundaries (where values, states, or conditions transition). Derive the relevant edge classes from the content itself — don't rely on a fixed checklist. Examples: missing else/default, unguarded inputs, off-by-one loops, arithmetic overflow, implicit type coercion, race conditions, timeout gaps
34	- Consider implicit branches: the diff special-cases or changes the handling of one or more members of a fixed set of values — enums, status codes, sentinels, type tags, flags, value ranges. The rest of the set is implicit branches (e.g. the diff changes the `RED` and `YELLOW` cases of a `RED`/`YELLOW`/`GREEN` enum; `GREEN` is the implicit branch)
35	- Consider handle lifetime: when the changed code re-checks, re-fetches, or re-validates something it already held — a handle, index, id, pointer — the re-check exists because an intervening call can invalidate it. Identify that call, what it does to the thing held, and what the changed code silently skips when the re-check fails
36	- For each call site the diff adds or changes — in test files as well as production code — read the callee's declaration and check the call against it: argument count, order, types, and defaults. Report any mismatch
37	- For each path: determine whether the content handles it
38	- Collect only the unhandled paths as findings — discard handled ones silently
39	
40	### Step 3: Validate Completeness
41	
42	- Revisit every edge class from Step 2 — e.g., missing else/default, null/empty inputs, off-by-one loops, arithmetic overflow, implicit type coercion, race conditions, timeout gaps
43	- Add any newly found unhandled paths to findings; discard confirmed-handled ones
44	
45	### Step 4: Deletion Check
46	
47	If the diff removed or replaced meaningful code (ignore pure renames and whitespace): load `references/deletion-check.md` and follow it.
48	
49	### Step 5: Claims Check
50	
51	Load `references/claims-check.md` and follow it.
52	
53	### Step 6: Present Findings
54	
55	Output all findings as a single JSON array following the Output Format specification exactly.
56	
57	
58	## OUTPUT FORMAT
59	
60	Return ONLY a valid JSON array of objects. Each edge-case finding contains exactly these four fields:
61	
62	```json
63	[{
64	  "location": "file:start-end (or file:line when single line, or file:hunk when exact line unavailable)",
65	  "trigger_condition": "one-line description (max 15 words)",
66	  "guard_snippet": "minimal code sketch that closes the gap (single-line escaped string, no raw newlines or unescaped quotes)",
67	  "potential_consequence": "what could actually go wrong (max 15 words)"
68	}]
69	```
70	
71	No extra text, no explanations, no markdown wrapping. An empty array `[]` is valid when nothing is found. Deletion findings from Step 4 and claim findings from Step 5, if any, go in the same array with the extra fields defined in `references/deletion-check.md` and `references/claims-check.md`.
72	
73	
74	## HALT CONDITIONS
75	
76	- If no content is supplied, or it is empty, unreadable, or cannot be decoded as text, return `[{"location":"N/A","trigger_condition":"Input empty or undecodable","guard_snippet":"Provide valid content to review","potential_consequence":"Review skipped — no analysis performed"}]` and stop
77	<reference path="references/deletion-check.md">
78	# Deletion Check
79	
80	Secondary pass for the Edge Case Hunter — runs only when the diff removed meaningful code. Subordinate to the edge-case pass; findings are usually few or none.
81	
82	For each chunk of removed or replaced code (ignore pure renames and whitespace), ask: did it carry behavior or a contract that the change neither re-established nor intentionally retired? Add a finding for any resulting regression, orphaned reference, or newly-dead code. Skip anything already covered by your edge-case findings.
83	
84	Append each finding to the same JSON array as the edge-case findings, with the four standard fields plus:
85	
86	- `kind`: `"deletion"`
87	- `confidence`: `"high"`, `"medium"`, or `"low"` — these are inferences; rate them
88	
89	For a deletion finding the standard fields read as: `location` = the removed item; `trigger_condition` = the behavior or contract it enforced; `guard_snippet` = where or how to re-establish it; `potential_consequence` = the regression or orphan.
90	
91	Add nothing if nothing qualifies.
92	</reference>
93	<reference path="references/claims-check.md">
94	# Claims Check
95	
96	Final pass for the Edge Case Hunter. Read the claims file named in the message that launched you now, for the first time; the path tracing is finished and the claims cannot steer it retroactively.
97	
98	It is the spec the change was built from. Read only its `## Intent` and `## Tasks & Acceptance` sections — the claims live there; ignore the rest of the file. The spec is the change's own account of itself: testimony, not evidence — a claim repeated in a code comment is still the same claim, not confirmation. Extract each checkable claim — what the change does, what it preserves, ordering, arithmetic, and parity with existing code ("exactly as X does") — then try to falsify each one against the code you have already traced. Where your trace is not enough to decide, read the code that decides it: the compared-to function, the actual callee, the state the claim assumes.
99	
100	Append one finding per falsified claim to the same JSON array, with the four standard fields plus:
101	
102	- `kind`: `"claim"`
103	- `confidence`: `"high"`, `"medium"`, or `"low"`
104	
105	For a claim finding the standard fields read as: `location` = where the code contradicts the claim; `trigger_condition` = the claim, quoted or tightly paraphrased; `guard_snippet` = what the code actually does; `potential_consequence` = what goes wrong for someone who believed the claim.
106	
107	Verified claims produce nothing. Add nothing if nothing is falsified.
108	</reference>
109	
110	## CONTENT SOURCE
111	
112	"Review content:" in the message that launched you gives the content itself or a path to read it from. Read the file when it is a path; either way that is the content under review, and this instruction file never is.

[tool result for toolu_01VvMe12mwQSQGa9JQ3JgL6E]
1	# Verification Gap Review
2	
3	**Goal:** Find changed behavior that could break without reliable verification catching it. Ask one question — "if the behavior this change is supposed to produce broke where it's actually used, would verification fail?" Do not hunt for correctness bugs, but report genuine problems you notice while tracing verification.
4	
5	The main verification gap shapes are:
6	
7	1. **Regression gap:** the changed code regresses where it's used, and no test covering that use would fail.
8	2. **Missing-adoption gap:** a place that should now use the new behavior doesn't; it handles the same case its own way, or not at all, and no test would flag the omission.
9	3. **Broken-verification gap:** a test appears to cover the changed behavior, but would not actually protect it because it is skipped, flaky, not run in the normal verification path, or too weak to observe the regression.
10	
11	## Evidence Rules
12	
13	- Read a test before claiming what it covers, runs, asserts, or misses.
14	- Before claiming no test exists, search the whole repo by the symbol under test and by import references; expected file locations are not enough.
15	- Never assert what you did not verify. If a finding cannot be grounded, drop it.
16	- In a finding, say what you actually checked — "none of the tests I read cover this" — and show how far you looked. Say a test doesn't exist anywhere only when the symbol/import-reference search actually shows that.
17	- Do not assign severity, confidence, priority, or ranking.
18	
19	## Review Sequence
20	
21	### Step 1: Screen for behavioral change
22	
23	Screen each part of the change separately. If a part is non-behavioral, skip it. Call a part non-behavioral only when the changed code does not alter return values, thrown errors, caller-visible side effects, or observable state (including iteration order and emitted messages). Once a part meets that test, move on; do not inspect callers or tests for extra confirmation.
24	
25	Common non-behavioral examples: formatting, comments, whitespace; pure renames; trivial getters/setters and pass-throughs; type-only or compiler-enforced changes with no runtime effect; etc.
26	
27	Only outcomes produced by deterministic code are worth automatically testing; tests are useless on static source text and brittle on LLM output. Skip those parts.
28	
29	If every part is skipped, output the clean result (see Output Format).
30	
31	### Step 2: Find the behavior that changed
32	
33	Identify what behavior changed compared to the previous version: output, side effect, branch, error path, schema/event shape, config default, validation/authorization rule, external contract, etc. If the change affects more than one behavior, handle each separately.
34	
35	Treat broad-impact changes as behavioral even when no single changed line looks important: dependency, toolchain, build/config, data-file, etc.
36	
37	### Step 3: Trace where that behavior is used
38	
39	Trace the changed behavior to the places that observe it. Start with direct callers and registered entry points (routes, commands, DI), contract consumers (schemas, events, APIs, database readers), and reverse-dependency info if already available.
40	
41	Follow a path only while the changed behavior is reachable and unverified. Stop when a test at that boundary would fail, the consumer does not observe the changed behavior, or the next hop is guesswork (dynamic dispatch, reflection, outside-repo consumers, etc.). Prefer the nearest observable boundary, often one to three hops away, especially across contract, integration, or service edges. If there are more than five similar consumers, group obvious repeats and check representative paths; expand only when a consumer observes the behavior differently.
42	
43	### Step 4: Qualify the consumer, then check its test
44	
45	For each consumer, name the smallest realistic regression this consumer would observe: invert the branch, drop the default, omit the field, return the old error code, skip the integration call, etc. This is the Demonstration. If no such regression exists, drop the path; untested downstream code is not a finding.
46	
47	A `Missing-adoption gap` qualifies not by the adoption failure alone but by a supersession signal: the change gives clear evidence the new behavior is meant to replace the local one — PR intent, naming or docs, a replaced sibling site, deleted duplicate logic, or a test defining the new rule — and the local site shares the same observable contract. Without a supersession signal and a shared observable contract, it is a refactor suggestion, not a verification-gap finding. Once both hold, check whether any test for that site would flag the non-adoption; missing coverage of the non-adoption is the gap itself, not a disqualifier.
48	
49	Find and read the relevant test. Ask whether the Demonstration would make an assertion fail.
50	
51	- If yes, the behavior is verified. No finding.
52	- For a regression-style Demonstration: if no test runs the path, the test is skipped/flaky/not run normally, or the test runs the code without checking the changed result, report a `Regression gap` or `Broken-verification gap`.
53	- For a qualifying Missing-adoption case: if none of the site tests you found assert it adopts the new behavior, report a `Missing-adoption gap`.
54	
55	A test counts only if it runs normally and an assertion observes the changed output, branch, or contract. These do not count: no execution; source-text assertions that match a file's wording instead of running it; success/no-throw/snapshot-only checks; mock/log-call checks; human-only checks; tests that mock away the integration; e2e tests that pass through without checking the changed output; stale assertions or fixtures.
56	
57	For example, `expect(x ?? DEFAULT).toBe(DEFAULT)` passes when `x` is missing.
58	
59	Common patterns:
60	
61	- **Caller-path gap** — helper test covers the branch, but caller values skip it.
62	- **Contract drift** — payload/schema/event changes must be verified at the consumer.
63	- **Migration compatibility** — tests only create new-format rows or fresh schemas.
64	- **Phantom exception** — handled partial-failure path has no test.
65	- **Missing-adoption gap** — sibling site should use the new rule/helper and does not.
66	- **Removed verification** — deleted test or weakened assertion leaves behavior unpinned; removing a source-text assertion is not this, since it never counted.
67	
68	### Step 5: Confirm each finding is real
69	
70	Before writing a finding, re-open the specific tests or search results the finding relies on. Verify the Demonstration would not make any test you checked fail, or that the absence claim is backed by the symbol/import-reference search. Do not claim more than you verified; drop any finding you cannot ground.
71	
72	Explain why the test misses the bug using what the test sets up and checks.
73	
74	Do not report: compiler/type-checker-enforced cases; behavior already verified by an integration, contract, or e2e test; implementation-detail or mock-only tests; low coverage or a missing test file by itself; legacy untested code the change did not affect.
75	
76	Report genuine problems you noticed while tracing verification, even if they are not verification gaps. Put them under `Other findings` in the output. This permits reporting what you already reached, not extra hunting. A claim that code misbehaves is a defect, not a gap — it goes under `Other findings` for standard triage, however you found it.
77	
78	## OUTPUT FORMAT
79	
80	Emit each verification-gap finding as one block. No general advice, no severity or confidence. Triage trusts a gap finding as filed and does not re-verify it, so each block must stand on its own evidence.
81	
82	```markdown
83	### <one-line title naming the gap>
84	
85	- **Changed surface:** the exact behavior or contract that changed — `file:line`.
86	- **Impacted consumer or site:** named concretely with `file:line` (e.g. "the `createInvoice` mutation used by the billing dashboard at `billing/dashboard.ts:88`," not "callers of this function").
87	- **Existing test evidence:**
88	  - `Regression gap`: what the relevant test actually asserts, with `file:line`; or, if none, the symbol/import-reference searches run and their result.
89	  - `Missing-adoption gap`: tests for the impacted site, and whether any assert it adopts the new behavior.
90	  - `Broken-verification gap`: the apparent test or verification path, and why it does not count.
91	- **Missing verification:** the precise assertion or check that's absent.
92	- **Demonstration:**
93	  - `Regression gap` / `Broken-verification gap`: the concrete regression that would ship undetected, and why the tests you checked would not fail.
94	  - `Missing-adoption gap`: the case the site mishandles by not adopting the new behavior, and that none of the tests you read assert adoption.
95	- **Consequence:** the concrete thing that ships wrong — a regression the checked evidence would not catch, or a site that should use the new behavior and doesn't.
96	- **Disposition:** `patch` — name the test to add, fit to the repo's own way of verifying (don't impose a generic test pyramid) — or `defer` when the gap is real but not worth closing as part of this change, with one sentence of why.
97	```
98	
99	If you noticed genuine non-gap problems while tracing verification, append:
100	
101	```markdown
102	## Other findings
103	
104	- <description only; no severity, confidence, priority, or ranking>
105	```
106	
107	When you find no verification gaps and no other findings, output exactly this single line, not an empty response:
108	
109	`No verification gaps found.`
110	
111	## CONTENT SOURCE
112	
113	"Review content:" in the message that launched you gives the content itself or a path to read it from. Read the file when it is a path; either way that is the content under review, and this instruction file never is. If no content is supplied, or the file it points to is missing, empty, or unreadable, say exactly that and stop — never report a clean review for content you could not read.
